**English** | [繁體中文](developer-guide.zh-TW.md)

# Developer guide

How the code is organized, how to run and test it, and how to extend it. Read [Architecture](architecture.md) first for the big picture.

## Stack

| Layer | Tech |
| --- | --- |
| Language | TypeScript (strict), ES modules |
| Server | Node.js ≥ 22.13, [Hono](https://hono.dev) on `@hono/node-server`, run with `tsx` (no build step) |
| Database | SQLite via the built-in `node:sqlite` (`DatabaseSync`) |
| API keys | OS credential store via [`@napi-rs/keyring`](https://github.com/Brooooooklyn/keyring-node) (prebuilt binaries, no compiler needed) |
| Client | React 19, Vite, `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` + `rehype-highlight` |
| Tests | Vitest |

## Project layout

```text
src/
  shared/types.ts          types shared by server and client (+ PROVIDERS, effortsFor…)
  shared/mentions.ts       the @mention rule shared by the server and the UI (Markdown prose only), and the names no member has
  shared/links.ts          where a bare URL ends, also right before Chinese text written after it (server and UI)
  shared/search.ts         search words, matches ignoring case, excerpts, highlighting (server and UI)
  shared/family.ts         families of branched conversations: each one's parent and top, also once the top is deleted (server and UI)
  shared/auth.ts           signing a browser in (server and UI): one-time codes (Crockford base 32), the link's #code= fragment, a browser's label, the key header
  shared/share.ts          a shared page's placeholder links to the files that go along (written by the UI, filled in by the server), and sizes as the share limits count them
  shared/schedule.ts       when a schedule sends next: wall-clock times in a time zone, clock changes (server and UI)
  shared/notify.ts         alerts: what changed in a conversation that the user is told of (a reply done, needs you), and its text's detail (server and UI)
  server/
    index.ts               wiring, static files, startup and shutdown
    web.ts                 serves the built UI: hashed assets cached for good, index.html checked every time; where it is (RELAY_WEB_DIR)
    parent.ts              the desktop app that started the server, if any: tells it the server listens (or cannot), hears it ask for a shutdown, and what it says of its updates
    updates.ts             the desktop app's updates as the chat shows them: what the app last said, and the chat's asks (check now, update) passed on to it
    app.ts                 createApp(): routes, security middleware (Host/Origin, JSON writes, sign-in), SSE (testable with app.request())
    auth.ts                signed-in browsers: one-time codes, sessions (a cookie and a key, only their hashes stored), wrong-code limit
    push.ts                push notifications: browsers' subscriptions, VAPID keys (RFC 8292), message encryption (RFC 8291), sending to push services
    alerts.ts              tells subscribed browsers when agents finish or need the user, holding them while the browser's page is in front
    signin.ts              the sign-in link the server and `npm run login` print
    protect.ts             what agents below full access may not reach: the browsers' folders and the database with the secrets' key file (protectedReads), Harness Relay's own folder in edit mode
    orchestrator.ts        team turns: who speaks next, prompt delivery, streaming into messages; auto team, resend
    planner.ts             auto team: lead-agent prompt (question, history, current team, each CLI's models and tools, the tool library), reply parsing, runnable team, fallback;
                           changing a team at the user's request (revise prompt, `decideRevision`)
    roleGuides.ts          how each model family takes a role best, for the roles the lead agent writes (only the families its CLIs can run)
    grouping.ts            sidebar groups: the lead agent sorts a new conversation by its first message
    scheduler.ts           schedules: sends each conversation's scheduled message when it comes due, missed times, notes
    prompt.ts              role briefing, turn prompt, @mention parsing, [PASS]
    approvals.ts           "always allow" rule keys (one per command the line runs)
    commands.ts            reads a shell command line: simple commands, sh -c, wrappers, publish check
    i18n.ts                server messages (zh-TW / en), language per request and run
    agents/
      types.ts             AgentRunner interface, AgentEvent, helpers
      claude.ts            Claude Code: stream-json both ways, can_use_tool control requests
      codex.ts             Codex: `codex app-server` (thread / turn / approval requests)
      grok.ts              Grok: ACP (`grok agent stdio`)
      cursor.ts            Cursor: ACP (`cursor-agent acp`), no OS sandbox; a private config folder per run (its settings, deny rules, a link to the account's sessions)
      antigravity.ts       Antigravity: ACP (`agy acp`), no sandbox (below full access every command asks; a refusal ends its turn); settings of its own per run (`--cli_dir_name`: the account's model and service only), a project's own MCP servers, hooks and plugins asked about before it starts, the commands it leaves running ended after it, the account's home (`--gemini-dir`), the briefing in its system prompt
      copilot.ts           Copilot: ACP (`copilot --acp`), its own sandbox below full access (`--sandbox`, its policy checked before each turn; a refusal ends its turn); a home of its own per run (`prepareCopilotRun`: Harness Relay's settings, the account's sign-in state, links to its sessions (`session-state`, and on macOS and Linux its `session-store.db`, whose usage AgentsView reads for token counts) and what it loads; in Copilot's usual home, `~/.agents/skills` as a skill folder), what decides how agents run later and the shared caches read-only in its sandbox, a project's own hooks asked about before it starts (and its sandbox checked first, in a home where nothing runs, when a session would run something by itself), the user's own endpoints through its BYOK variables, the briefing in its system prompt (`copilot-instructions.md`)
      muse.ts              Muse Code: ACP (`muse acp`), in its own OS sandbox below full access; a config folder of its own per run (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, in the data folder's `muse-runs`: an allowlist of the account's settings with Harness Relay's permission profile, from `:ask-me` with a deny rule per protected path; a link to the account's sign-in, and to its skills with a copy of its rules (`AGENTS.md`); a `trust.json` marking the working directories untrusted below full access), the session's mode per permission, a command's network always asked of the user, a session resumed only with the permission and protected paths it was made with, its approval judge off, the briefing as its developer prompt, the empty folders its Linux sandbox leaves in the working directories removed
      acp.ts               JSON-RPC peer used by the Codex, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code adapters and the generic ACP turn, and the ACP helpers Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code share (prompt blocks, MCP servers, permission answers, content text)
      acpTurn.ts           the open agents' generic ACP turn: session updates → events, permission → decision → approval, resume, model / effort / mode, per-CLI hooks (`AcpAgentSpec`)
      open.ts              what the open agents share around their process: the run's backend, their home (state and config paths), the run's temp folder, the relay sandbox (`startOpenProcess`)
      opencode.ts goose.ts cline.ts openhands.ts
                           the ACP open agents, one file each: their `AcpAgentSpec` and `OpenAgentDefinition`
      pi.ts                Pi over its JSONL RPC (`pi --mode rpc`)
      piGate.js            Pi's gate extension (loaded by Pi below full access): asks before each tool call over the RPC
      index.ts             runner registry, and `openAgents`
    process.ts             spawn/kill helpers (process groups, line streaming, raw-byte capture, timeouts), runSync() for a quick probe the server waits for, converse() and converseFramed() for a short JSON-RPC exchange (JSON lines, or LSP's Content-Length frames: FrameDecoder)
    winLaunch.ts           on Windows, what to start for a CLI that is a .cmd (npm's shims, Cursor's and Muse Code's launchers)
    ports.ts               Harness Relay's ports: 8787 (npm start, the desktop app), 8789 and Vite's 5173 (npm run dev), PORT over either
    config.ts              what the environment sets at startup: accounts per CLI, team size, the turn timeout, voice input's download folders and its own folder (checked; the server refuses to start on a value that is not valid)
    share.ts               sharing a conversation: the page as a secret gist through the user's gh (create, delete), the gistpreview link; the files its replies link to (found as the download route finds them, of any kind, named apart) in a second secret gist that git pushes them into, signed in by gh, found again by its token when gh cannot say it made it
    about.ts               which Harness Relay this is and on what (its version, the system, Node), for a problem report: nothing that names the user
    screenshots.ts         a problem report's screenshots: checked to be PNG, JPEG or WebP images (no GIF), named by their place, without their metadata (a photo's location), uploaded as one secret gist through the user's gh
    providers.ts           CLI status, login sessions and logout per account, model cache, each account's plan usage (kept a while)
    login.ts               a CLI's own sign-in run without a terminal (CliLogin): its output, links and codes, input, timeout; the CLIs' in Settings and gh's for sharing
    usage.ts               each account's plan usage, asked of its CLI without a prompt (Claude Code's get_usage, Codex's account/rateLimits/read, Grok's _x.ai/billing, muse usage --json, Copilot's account.getQuota on its headless server, Antigravity's /usage and /credits in print mode, Cursor's plan from about); none for an account on a key its provider bills by use
    models.ts              model / effort discovery per CLI
    db.ts                  open SQLite, migrations, transaction(), the data folder's lock (lockDataDir)
    store.ts               conversations, messages, agent sessions, presets, sidebar groups, search, schedules
    settings.ts            provider settings, each CLI's accounts, default cwd, the CLIs' own homes, child-process env
    agentsview.ts          AgentsView: each conversation sent as one thread, the agents' sessions linked under it (Cursor's and the open agents' as Harness Relay recorded them)
    secrets.ts             OS credential store for API keys (keyring), availability check; without one, the database encrypted with the key in secrets.key (openSecretStore)
    backends.ts            the open agents' model backends: API keys and custom endpoints (keyring, `model_backends`) and the deleted endpoints' ids (`deleted_endpoints`), `backendRun()` for one run; `endpointRunModel()` for a run of Claude Code, Codex, Grok or Copilot on an endpoint, or refused on a deleted one
    relaySandbox.ts        Harness Relay's own sandbox around the open agents below full access (`sandboxCommand`, srt)
    relaySandboxHost.ts    the host process each sandboxed run gets: one sandbox-runtime for that run, its proxy and its control channel (fd 3)
    sandboxProcs.ts        a relay-sandbox run's leftover processes on macOS, found by their sandbox (sandbox_check)
    fsRemove.ts            removing what a run left without following links; the owner's permissions given back only at a start whose sweeps found nothing left
    tools.ts               the tool library: MCP servers (secrets in the keyring), skills (SKILL.md front matter), memory; what each agent gets
    files.ts               browsing a conversation's files: roots, listing, preview, path checks, what a folder's ZIP holds
    zip.ts                 a ZIP file written as it is sent (deflate, data descriptors, UTF-8 names)
    folderPicker.ts        the system's own folder dialog for the working directory box (AppleScript, zenity / kdialog, Windows Forms): which one, its command, its answer
    native.ts              what each CLI loads by itself (its skills, MCP servers and their tools, built-in tools, hooks, subagents, plugins, instruction and memory files), asked without a prompt (Cursor's read from the files it reads, as are Antigravity's MCP servers and instruction files, Copilot's hooks and custom agents, and Muse Code's MCP servers and hooks), and what Harness Relay's runs leave out of it
    grokTrust.ts           records Grok's trust in a folder (as grok --trust does), when the user confirms
    attachments.ts         files attached to messages: storage, safe names, images for the agents
    presets.ts             built-in team templates, in both languages
    validate.ts            input validation for agents, settings, schedules and model backends
    voice.ts               voice input: whisper.cpp engine and model installs (pinned, SHA-256 checked), the engine process, transcription
    workspace.ts           git: auto init, snapshots, worktrees, diffs, apply
    errors.ts              HttpError (user-facing errors with a status)
    hub.ts                 SSE fan-out
  web/
    main.tsx App.tsx api.ts util.ts copy.ts styles.css index.html
    auth.ts                the key a signed-in browser sends (localStorage, this origin only); what an answer "signed out" means (after the sign-ins on their way in this tab: signed out only for the key the request was sent with, which stays in the storage for the other tabs; sent again, once, with a key a sign-in put in its place; else left alone); another tab keeping a new key (`keyArrived`)
    signin.ts              signing in with a link (the code taken out of the address, the page's first check, shared by both runs of App's effect in development) and where the sign-in page points
    public/                copied into the build as it is: manifest.webmanifest and the home-screen icons (npm run icons), and sw.js, the service worker that shows push notifications (written by hand)
    alerts.ts              notifications and sound when agents finish or need the user (Settings → Notifications and sound): the browser's choices, what it can do, what the page does in each situation, one tab per alert, the push subscription kept in step, the sound
    chat.ts                ChatView's pure logic: stream reconnect decision, Apply availability, team-drawer draft and the team it edits, when the messages follow new output
    sidebar.ts             the sidebar's pure logic: conversations in their groups, each family of branches as a tree, pending moves, a collapsed group's or family's state
    schedule.ts            the schedule drawer's pure logic: the form, its problems, the next times, a schedule in a few words
    templates.ts           the template editor's pure logic: whether a template was changed
    files.ts               the file browser's pure logic: breadcrumb, filter, code language, line count, a folder's ZIP choice
    cwds.ts                the working directories box's pure logic: which working directories in use match what is typed (`~` as the home folder, `/` and `\` alike), how its list answers the keys and its arrow button, and its chips (adding each folder once, Enter and Backspace, where the folder dialog opens)
    download.ts            whether a file a reply links to can be downloaded (a HEAD request, a moment after it appears), and what a click does while it is missing
    share.ts               a shared page's DOM-free parts: what it leaves out, the image budget, the document and its CSP, the Harness Relay bar; the files its replies link to (read from download links, what each link becomes, which are chosen and kept across checks, which look secret)
    update.ts              the desktop app's updates in the chat: the dialog offering a new version once (in the app's own window, while no other dialog is open), Settings' Version card, how often to ask; and the page's watch on the server's version, which reloads a page open across an update
    dialogs.ts             the dialogs open on the page: only the top one takes keys (useDialog), and how many are open
    star.ts                Harness Relay's repository on GitHub (REPO, REPO_URL), which the star dialog opens: the landing page's REPO
    report.ts              a problem report by email: the mail's subject and text, the details that go along, the link to its screenshots, the mailto and Gmail links (and the clipboard for a report too long for them)
    sharePage.tsx          makes a shared page from the chat on screen (copy, strip, styles, images as data URLs within 1 MB, placeholder links to the files that go along); the file links of the shared messages
    tools.ts               the team editor's tool picker: library items, defaults per CLI, an agent's exceptions
    backends.ts            Settings → Model backends: the endpoint form's pure logic (an id from the name, the models typed, its problems)
    native.ts              NativeSetupPanel's pure logic: each CLI's tabs and their counts, ticking single items, what its runs leave out, whether it changed
    usage.ts               UsagePanel's pure logic: a limit's name, what is left of it, its bar's colour, when it starts over, the credits, why an account has no limits
    route.ts               the client's routes in the URL hash: #/ (home), #/new (New chat), #/schedule, #/c/<id> and #/settings/<section>
    voice.ts               voice input's pure logic: WAV encoding, silence, inserting the transcript, install progress
    recorder.ts            microphone recording in the browser, decoded to 16 kHz mono
    useVoice.ts            the voice status hook (polls while an install runs)
    i18n.tsx               language detection, provider, useT(); each language's messages loaded when needed (loadMessages)
    highlight.ts           the file browser's highlighting, with the highlighter replies' code blocks use
    tour.ts                whether this browser has seen the guided tour (the tour itself loads when it opens)
    chunks.ts              a part of the page loaded when first needed that could not be (a page open across an update): the page reloads once for it, not again within a minute (loadPart)
    limits.ts              the server's limits (GET /api/limits: accounts per CLI, team size, the turn timeout), the defaults until they arrive, asked again when a component opens, a chat's live updates reconnect, the sidebar reaches the server again or the page is shown again (only while a component shows them: never on the sign-in page)
    refresh.ts             the CLIs' status as the server checked it last (newerStatuses), and loads of what it does not date, such as the model catalog, in the order asked for (latestLoader)
    theme.ts               the light or dark theme: the system's, or the one chosen in Settings → Appearance (data-theme, kept in localStorage)
    locales/               zh-TW.ts (source of the message shape), en.ts
    components/            Sidebar, SidebarGroups (groups, drag and drop), SidebarSearch,
                           Home (the animated logo and the tagline, where the app opens), NewChat, TemplateEditor (the template dialog), TeamEditor, TeamReviser (the team
                           drawer's "Ask the lead agent"), ModelPicker, CliPicker (a choice of CLI: one row that scrolls sideways), ChatView,
                           MessageItem (incl. plan card), ChangesCard, Composer, Markdown,
                           SettingsPage, UsagePanel (an account's plan usage in Settings), CopyButton, Logo, Onboarding (guided tour),
                           MemberDots, icons, VoiceInput (microphone, setup dialog, install progress),
                           VoiceSettings (Settings → Voice input), ToolsSettings (Settings → Tool library),
                           BackendsSettings (Settings → Model backends),
                           SessionsSettings (Settings → Agent sessions),
                           SharesSettings (Settings → Shared conversations), ShareDialog (copy or share a conversation),
                           SignIn (the sign-in page), BrowsersSettings (Settings → Signed-in browsers),
                           NotificationsSettings (Settings → Notifications and sound),
                           GhHelp (what to do when the GitHub CLI is missing or not signed in, and its sign-in from there), LoginLinks (a sign-in's link and code, there and in Settings),
                           FileBrowser (the Files dialog), NativeSetupPanel (what a CLI loads by itself),
                           Deferred (a part loaded when first shown, lazyPart: a note to reload the page when it cannot be loaded or drawn),
                           GrokTrust (Grok's trust in a folder), SectionTitle,
                           CwdInput (the working directories box: a chip per folder, the Enter key after what is typed, the folder button, and the list of the working directories in use),
                           ScheduleDrawer (a conversation's schedule), ScheduleForm (the schedule's form, shared),
                           NewSchedule (the New schedule page),
                           ReportDialog (the sidebar's Report a problem), StarDialog (the star beside it: star the repository on GitHub),
                           VersionSettings (Settings → Version), UpdateDialog (a new version, offered once),
                           useDialog (focus handling for dialogs)
test/                      Vitest tests; fixtures/<agent>/ holds messages recorded from the open agents', Antigravity's, Copilot's and Muse Code's real CLIs (fixtures/antigravity/cli/ and fixtures/muse/cli/: agy's and muse's own listings; fixtures/copilot/cli/: copilot's; fixtures/usage/: each CLI's answer about an account's plan usage), fake-*.ts the stand-ins that replay them (fake-antigravity.ts for antigravity.test.ts, fake-copilot.ts for copilot.test.ts, fake-muse.ts for muse.test.ts and Muse Code's sign-in in providers.test.ts); setup.ts and timeouts.ts: how long tests wait (longer on Windows); orchestrator-harness.ts: the fake CLI the orchestrator's tests share
e2e/                       end-to-end tests (Playwright): npm run test:e2e
  README.md                the scenarios they cover, spec by spec (README.zh-TW.md in Chinese)
  fake-cli/fake-cli.cjs    the fake CLIs' entry: which of the twelve this process is, by the name it runs under
  fake-cli/agents/         one fake per CLI (<provider>.cjs), in its own protocol; opencode-goose.cjs what those two share
  fake-cli/lib/            what the fakes share: ACP and JSON-RPC on stdio, a turn's steps (plan.cjs: the e2e: directives), homes, files, the log
  fake-cli/fake-dialog.cjs the fake folder dialog (zenity, for Linux)
  support/                 a server for each test and its fake model endpoint (relay.ts), installing the fakes, bundled into one script (fake-cli.ts), the UI steps (app.ts), whether Harness Relay's own sandbox works here (sandbox.ts)
  support/clis.ts          the CLIs as the specs see them: eachCli(), the two teams (HALVES, eachHalf()), the CLIs a test server has (withClis()), the systems a CLI's tests run on (E2E_SYSTEM_CLIS), the permission each runs with here, skips (needsBelowFull, needsRelaySandbox), homes and sessions
  support/clis/            each CLI's facts for the specs (<provider>.ts, changed with its fake), all.ts gathers them
  fixtures.ts              the relay and page fixtures; global-setup.ts builds the UI (and a development build of it) and installs the fakes
  specs/                   the tests
  live/                    the live tests: the real CLIs on real models (npm run test:e2e:live; never on CI)
playwright.config.ts       the end-to-end tests' configuration (E2E_BROWSERS)
playwright.live.config.ts  the live tests' configuration
.gitattributes             every text file checked out with LF, also on Windows
.devcontainer/             the dev container for GitHub Codespaces (docs/codespaces.md): setup.sh installs the sandboxes' packages and the seven CLIs that are not open source, start.sh starts the server on the codespace's address
.github/workflows/ci.yml   CI: typecheck, tests and build on pull requests and pushes to main, on Linux and Windows; the desktop app packaged for Linux and checked
.github/workflows/e2e.yml  the end-to-end tests on Linux, macOS and Windows
.github/workflows/pages.yml  publishes site/ to GitHub Pages
.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/    the issue forms (bug report, feature request; both languages in each) and the links beside them
.github/pull_request_template.md  what a pull request's description covers (both languages)
scripts/preview-site.ts    npm run site:preview: serves site/ locally like Pages
scripts/sandbox-check.ts   npx tsx scripts/sandbox-check.ts: real checks of Harness Relay's own sandbox on this computer (not part of npm test)
scripts/app-icons.ts       npm run icons: draws the home-screen icons and writes the web app manifest
scripts/release.ts         the release version rules and the notes' list of changes: npm run release:next, release:check, release:notes, the Release workflow
.github/workflows/release.yml  the Release workflow (run by hand): checks, five installers, one GitHub release (and a copy of each for the app's updates, in its own repository)
scripts/demo/              npm run demo: records the demo again and updates its videos, stills and chapter rails
site/                      the landing page (static HTML, English + 繁體中文); DESIGN-GUIDELINES.md is not served
mirror/                    the website's download mirror: a Cloudflare Worker (worker.ts) in front of an R2 bucket, and its
                           wrangler.toml; deployed by hand (Releasing, "The download mirror")
docs/                      documentation (English + 繁體中文)
data/                      runtime data (relay.db) — git-ignored
desktop/                   the desktop app (Electron), a package of its own: npm install --prefix desktop
  README.md                the app's page for developers (README.zh-TW.md in Chinese)
  src/main.ts              the app: its window, its tray menu, starting and stopping the server, open at login, sharing with
                           the tailnet, updates, and its own check (--smoke-test); the only file that imports electron
  src/supervisor.ts        starts, watches and stops the server process (tested with a fake one)
  src/env.ts               the login shell's environment, and the server's (data folder, port, names it answers to)
  src/tailscale.ts         finding tailscale, reading its status and serve config, tailscale serve --bg
  src/autostart.ts         open at login on Linux (an XDG autostart entry)
  src/prefs.ts             the app's own settings (desktop.json)
  src/i18n.ts              the app's menu and dialogs in both languages
  src/icons.ts             the app and tray icons, drawn at build time from the mark
  src/manifest.ts          the package.json of the app folder Electron runs
  src/lifecycle.ts         the app's decisions without Electron: opening it again, crash notices, the chat's address, login items,
                           where the window's links go (guardContents) and what its pages may use, the tailnet name
  src/profile.ts           the window's browser folder (browser/), kept from agents; an old sign-in beside it removed
  src/log.ts               the server's log, started over past 5 MB (also while it runs)
  src/update.ts            updates to GitHub's newest release (a release candidate too, for an app that is one): the check, the download and its SHA-256,
                           the wait for the conversations, putting it in place
  src/updateSource.ts      where the app downloads its updates from: a repository and, for a private one, a token, fixed when it is built;
                           the Release workflow's checks that the repository can be read before anything is built, that the installers
                           were built with them, and that an app can download the copies
  src/traymenu.ts          the tray menu while it is open: changed in place, and opened again to show what changed
  src/selftest.ts          what the app needs of the server over HTTP, a question to the fake CLIs included (smoke.ts and the app's --smoke-test)
  scripts/build.ts         bundles the server and the app into desktop/build/ (as RELAY_VERSION, for a release; RELAY_UPDATES_REPO, RELAY_UPDATES_TOKEN)
  scripts/smoke.ts         runs the bundled server as the app does and checks it (CI, Release workflow)
  scripts/dist.ts          npm run desktop:dist: the installer, signed ad hoc, and what to double-click
  scripts/check-package.ts checks the packaged app: its fuses, its resources, no token in the server's files nor as text in the app's, the update source its environment names (encoded), and its own check (--smoke-test)
  scripts/packaged.ts      finds the packaged apps electron-builder left next to the installers
  entitlements.mac.plist   the Mac app's entitlements (the microphone, for voice input)
  macos/                   the microphone's reason in each language (InfoPlist.strings)
  scripts/parent-port.mjs  Electron's process.parentPort over Node's IPC, for smoke.ts and the tests
  electron-builder.yml     the installers (npm run desktop:dist), into desktop/release/
```

## Running

```bash
npm install
npm run dev        # tsx watch (API :8789) + vite (UI :5173, proxies /api/ to the API); prints a sign-in link for :5173
npm run typecheck
npm test
npm run test:e2e   # end-to-end tests in a browser, with fake CLIs (npm run test:e2e:install once)
E2E_LIVE_OLLAMA=http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1 npm run test:e2e:live   # the same with the real CLIs on your own models (see Live tests)
npm run build      # UI → dist/web
npm start          # one process: API + built UI on :8787; prints a sign-in link
npm run login      # another one-time sign-in link (ten minutes)
npx tsx scripts/sandbox-check.ts   # Harness Relay's own sandbox for real on this computer (macOS; Linux with bubblewrap, socat, ripgrep)
                                   # --only <text>: the checks whose name contains it; --keep: keep its scratch folder
                                   # run it after changing relaySandbox.ts, relaySandboxHost.ts, sandboxProcs.ts or an adapter's sandbox needs

npm install --prefix desktop   # once, for the desktop app only: Electron and electron-builder
npm run desktop                # build the UI and the desktop app, and run it without installing it
npm run desktop:dist           # …and make the installer for this system in desktop/release/
npm run desktop:dist -- --output <folder>   # …elsewhere (the `--` hands the option to the script)
npx tsx desktop/scripts/check-package.ts    # check the packaged app, as a release is checked
npm run desktop:typecheck
```

`npm run build` puts the libraries the page loads at startup in their own chunks (`react`, `highlight`, `vendor`), so a browser keeps them across app updates; Mermaid stays lazy. It warns when a chunk loaded at startup passes 500 kB (`vite.config.ts`); Mermaid's lazy chunks are larger and only load when a diagram needs them. What the page does not need to show itself loads when first opened, not at startup: Settings and its panels and the guided tour (`React.lazy` in `App.tsx`), and the dialogs a chat or page opens (the file browser, a schedule, sharing, a template, reporting a problem); keep a large view that opens on demand lazy too. Only the language the page shows is loaded, before it is drawn (`loadMessages()` in `src/web/i18n.tsx`; the other when chosen), its file asked for by the page's head along with the page's own (`languagePreload` in `vite.config.ts`, for the language saved in the browser). Make a lazy part with `lazyPart` and show it in `Deferred` (`src/web/components/Deferred.tsx`), not with a bare `React.lazy` and `Suspense`: a part that cannot be loaded, as in a page left open across an update, has the page reload once, the part waiting meanwhile (`loadPart` in `src/web/chunks.ts`, which `loadMessages` uses too), then says so with a button to reload, instead of an empty page; a part that fails to draw says that instead. Only those parts reload the page: Mermaid, also loaded on demand, shows a diagram's source when it cannot be loaded. There is one copy of highlight.js: the file browser highlights with lowlight, as replies' code blocks do (`src/web/highlight.ts`), and `highlight.js` (for its theme) stays on the range `lowlight` takes, or npm installs a second copy.

The web app manifest and the icons a phone puts on its home screen (or a browser shows for the installed app) are in `src/web/public/`. They are drawn by `npm run icons` (`scripts/app-icons.ts`) from the same three circles as the favicon and `Logo.tsx`, without an image library; run it again after changing the mark, the icons' sizes or the manifest, and commit what it writes. `src/web/public/sw.js`, the service worker that shows push notifications, is written by hand: plain JavaScript, not built, registered only once a browser turns notifications on ([Architecture → Notifications](architecture.md#notifications)).

`npm run dev` restarts the server on every file change. A restart aborts any running agent turn (its message is marked aborted), so avoid editing server files while a long turn is running.

### Environment variables

| Variable | Default | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PORT` | `8787` (`8789` with `npm run dev`) | API port (the Vite proxy uses it too). `npm run dev` has a port of its own so that it runs beside the desktop app, or an `npm start` of another checkout, which use 8787 (`src/server/ports.ts`); beside an `npm start` of the same checkout it refuses to start, since the two would share its data folder |
| `HOST` | `127.0.0.1` | Bind address |
| `RELAY_DATA_DIR` | `./data` | Where `relay.db` lives. One server holds it while it runs (`server.lock`): a second one with the same folder refuses to start. The relay sandbox keeps its sockets in it (`srt/`), so its path may be at most 70 bytes on macOS and 61 on Linux (UTF-8: a character outside ASCII takes 2 to 4) for the open agents to run below full access |
| `RELAY_DEFAULT_CWD` | process cwd | Initial default working directory (until changed in Settings) |
| `RELAY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` | — | Extra comma-separated host names accepted by the Host/Origin check, such as the machine's Tailscale name (see [Use it from your phone](remote-access.md)); in a GitHub codespace `.devcontainer/start.sh` adds its forwarded name ([Try it in GitHub Codespaces](codespaces.md)) |
| `RELAY_KEYRING` | — | `off` keeps API keys and MCP values in the database, encrypted with the key in `secrets.key`, instead of the OS credential store (e.g. for a throwaway data directory) |
| `RELAY_WEB_DIR` | `./dist/web` | Where the built UI is (the desktop app points it at its own copy) |
| `RELAY_KEYRING_SERVICE` | `harness-relay` | The name API keys are filed under in the credential store (the desktop app's own: `harness-relay-desktop`) |
| `RELAY_SECRETS_KEY_FILE` | `<data folder>/secrets.key` | Without a credential store: the file with the key the secrets in the database are encrypted with, 32 bytes in base64 or hex (`openssl rand -base64 32`), such as a systemd credential outside the data folder. It must be there: the server refuses to start without it (its own default one it makes). Agents below full access can neither read nor write it |
| `RELAY_APP_BROWSER_DIR` | — | Set by the desktop app: its window's browser folder (cookies, localStorage: its sign-in), which agents below full access may not read (`protectedReads()`) |
| `RELAY_UPDATES` | — | Read by the desktop app, not the server: `off` keeps it from asking GitHub for new releases |
| `RELAY_UPDATES_REPO` | `Agentic-System-Lab/harness-relay-updates` | Read when the desktop app is built (`desktop/scripts/build.ts`), never when it runs: the GitHub repository (`owner/name`) the app downloads its updates from, the installers' copies for updates; never this repository (the versions and their checksums still come from this repository's releases). The [Release workflow](#releasing) sets it from its `UPDATES_REPO` variable |
| `RELAY_UPDATES_TOKEN` | — | Read when the desktop app is built, never when it runs: a fine-grained personal access token (`github_pat_…`, no other kind) the app downloads those copies with on GitHub's API, for a private repository. It goes into the app, where anyone with a copy can read it: let it read that repository's contents and nothing else. The Release workflow sets it from its `UPDATES_READ_TOKEN` secret |
| `RELAY_MAX_ACCOUNTS` | `10` | Accounts per CLI, the default one included (1–100) |
| `RELAY_MAX_TEAM_AGENTS` | `8` | Most members a team may have, set by hand or by the lead agent (1–32). A team the lead agent designs by itself keeps to 5, or fewer when this is lower |
| `RELAY_TURN_TIMEOUT_MIN` | `45` | Each agent turn's safety timeout in minutes, a turn waiting for the user's answer included (1–1440) |
| `RELAY_VOICE_MODEL_BASE_URL` | Hugging Face, pinned to one commit | The http(s) folder voice input downloads its models from (`<url>/ggml-<model>.bin`), such as a mirror. Each file is still checked against its pinned size and SHA-256 |
| `RELAY_VOICE_ENGINE_BASE_URL` | whisper.cpp's release on GitHub | The http(s) folder voice input downloads its engine from on Linux and Windows (`<url>/<archive>`, the names of `ENGINE_BUILDS`), checked the same way |
| `RELAY_VOICE_DIR` | `<data folder>/voice` | Where voice input keeps its models and engine: an absolute path, or one starting with `~/`. Not in a temp folder (the server refuses to start), and edit mode is refused in a working directory that holds it or is inside it, since the engine runs outside any sandbox (so is it in the default folder). The engine's empty static folder stays in the data folder (`voice-public`). Removing voice input there removes the files Harness Relay downloads, by their names (the models, this version's engine), and the folders it made once they are empty; nothing else |

The server passes its own environment to the CLIs, minus Claude Code's nesting variables, plus stored API keys (`SettingsStore.env()`). Cursor also gets `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=memory` when it runs with a key or as an account besides the default one (`CURSOR_SHARED_SIGN_IN`: what it signs in with stays in that run, never over the computer user's one Cursor sign-in), and each Cursor run gets `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR` (its private config folder) and `NO_OPEN_BROWSER=1`. Antigravity has no variable for its home, so an account's is Harness Relay's own (`HARNESS_RELAY_ANTIGRAVITY_HOME`, the folder its `.gemini` is in, never passed on to agy): `agy acp` gets `--gemini-dir <home>/.gemini`, and the commands that only ask it something (`agy models`, `agy -p /skills`…, `agy acp --login`) get that folder as `HOME` (`USERPROFILE` on Windows), with `SSH_CONNECTION` set so that agy prints its sign-in link instead of opening a browser (`antigravityProbeEnv()`); the agent's own commands keep the user's `HOME`. An Antigravity account besides the default one gets neither `GEMINI_API_KEY` nor `AGY_ADC_AUTH` from the server's environment (`CREDENTIAL_ENV`), only its own key, and `HARNESS_RELAY_ANTIGRAVITY_KEY_ONLY=1` (not passed on to agy either), so that its runs and questions tell agy to use the key (`modelProvider: "gemini"`) and never fall back to the default account's Google sign-in. Each Copilot run gets `COPILOT_HOME` set to a home of the run's own (`prepareCopilotRun`, in Harness Relay's temporary folder), never `COPILOT_ALLOW_ALL` or `COPILOT_ASSISTED_APPROVAL`, and on an endpoint Copilot's BYOK variables (`COPILOT_PROVIDER_BASE_URL`, `COPILOT_PROVIDER_TYPE=openai`, `COPILOT_PROVIDER_WIRE_API=completions`, `COPILOT_MODEL`, and `COPILOT_PROVIDER_API_KEY` when the endpoint has a key) in place of any the server inherited; a Copilot account besides the default one gets none of `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` from the server's environment, only its own token. Muse Code has no variable for its home either, only the XDG ones, so an account's is Harness Relay's own too (`HARNESS_RELAY_MUSE_HOME`, the folder of its `config` and `data` folders, never passed on to muse): the commands that only ask it something (`muse usage`, `muse login`, `muse logout`) get those as `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` (`museProbeEnv()`); a run, and `muse skills list --json`, get the account's `data` folder as `XDG_DATA_HOME`, a config folder of their own as `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `MUSE_DISABLE_APPROVAL_JUDGE=1` (`museEnv()`), which reach the commands Muse runs too. A Muse Code account besides the default one gets no `META_API_KEY` from the server's environment (`CREDENTIAL_ENV`), only its own key, and `HARNESS_RELAY_MUSE_KEY_ONLY=1` (not passed on to muse either), so that its runs never link the default account's Meta sign-in. An open agent gets it without the model keys and base URLs the server inherited, nor Cursor's `CURSOR_API_KEY` and `CURSOR_AUTH_TOKEN`, nor Antigravity's `AGY_ADC_AUTH`, nor Copilot's `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN` and BYOK address and keys, nor Muse Code's `META_API_KEY` (`INHERITED_BACKEND_VARS`), with the key of its own run's backend alone and its home (`HARNESS_RELAY_AGENT_HOME`) instead, and below full access only inside the relay sandbox, which sets the proxy variables and its own `TMPDIR`.

### The desktop app

`desktop/` is the desktop app ([user guide](user-guide.md#desktop-app); its files, commands and checks at a glance in [`desktop/README.md`](../desktop/README.md)). It is a package of its own, with its own `package.json` and lockfile, so `npm install` at the root never downloads Electron (about 100 MB) for people who use `npm start`. Install it once with `npm install --prefix desktop`; it uses the root's `tsx` and `typescript`, so install the root first. Its code is plain TypeScript checked by `npm run desktop:typecheck`. The parts without Electron (`env.ts`, `supervisor.ts`, `tailscale.ts`, `autostart.ts`, `prefs.ts`, `profile.ts`, `log.ts`, `update.ts`, `updateSource.ts`, `traymenu.ts`, `selftest.ts`, `i18n.ts`, `icons.ts`, `manifest.ts`, `lifecycle.ts`, and `scripts/packaged.ts`) are tested from the root `test/` like the rest; only `main.ts` imports `electron`.

How it runs:

- **The server** is `src/server/index.ts`, bundled by esbuild into one ES module (`desktop/build/server/server.mjs`). It runs in an Electron utility process: Electron's own Node (Electron 45: Node 24, with `node:sqlite`), so the app needs no Node.js (except for Harness Relay's own sandbox: see below). The built UI (`server/web`, given as `RELAY_WEB_DIR`) and the keyring module (`server/node_modules/@napi-rs/`, native code, so it is copied, not bundled) sit next to it. In the installed app they are in the app's resources (`Resources/server` on macOS), outside the app archive, so `protect.ts`'s `RELAY_ROOT` is the app itself (`Harness Relay.app/Contents` on macOS, the install folder on Windows).
- **Messages** (`DesktopToServer` and `ServerToDesktop` in `src/shared/types.ts`, through `process.parentPort`; see `src/server/parent.ts`). The server says when it listens, or why it cannot. The app asks for a sign-in link (a new one-time code, one minute: `CODE_TTL_MS.desktop`) each time it opens the chat, how many conversations have agents at work (`busy`: `Orchestrator.busy()`, which counts runs and runs being set up, changes being made to a conversation such as a parallel reply applied, the lead agent changing a team or sorting a conversation into a group, and branches being made; an update waits for none), and for a shutdown, which runs the same shutdown as SIGTERM: agents are stopped and their process groups killed first. Windows has no signal to send, so this is how it stops there. A server the app started prints no sign-in code: its output goes to the app's log, which agents may read. It also tells the sign-in page so (`createApp({ desktopApp })`: `GET /api/auth` adds `desktop: true`, and the 401 message changes), which then points at the app's menu instead of a terminal: at **Open Harness Relay** in the app's window (Electron names itself in the user agent), at **Open in browser** in a browser (`src/web/signin.ts`). Started by `npm start` or `npm run dev`, the server has no parent and none of this happens.
- **The environment.** On macOS and Linux the app runs the user's login shell (`$SHELL -i -l -c`, printing `/usr/bin/env -0` between two marks) before it starts the server, at launch and again on **Restart server…** and **Start server**, and gives the server that environment, as a terminal would have it. On Windows it takes its own environment at launch; a restart or a start reads PATH and the user's variables again with PowerShell (`windowsEnv()`, `mergeWindowsEnv()`): PATH as Windows makes it for a new program (the system's, then the user's), followed by the app's own other entries; the system's other variables are left alone (some, such as `USERNAME`, are defaults the session replaces). If that fails it adds the usual install folders to PATH instead (`withCommonPaths`). `serverEnv()` then sets `RELAY_DATA_DIR` (the app's data folder, unless the user set one), `RELAY_KEYRING_SERVICE` (`harness-relay-desktop`, with the app's own data folder, which also leaves out a shell's `RELAY_SECRETS_KEY_FILE`, the key file of another data folder's database), `RELAY_DEFAULT_CWD` (the home folder, unless set), `RELAY_WEB_DIR`, `RELAY_APP_BROWSER_DIR` (the window's browser folder, below), `PORT` (from `desktop.json`), `HOST=127.0.0.1`, and `RELAY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` with this computer's tailnet name: as Tailscale reports it (also before it has connected, when it knows it), else the last one seen (`tailnetName` in `desktop.json`), so that a start at login before Tailscale connects still lets the phone in. It drops `ELECTRON_*` variables.
- **The supervisor** (`supervisor.ts`) checks that the port is free, starts the server, waits for it to listen (three minutes at most: after an update macOS may ask for the keychain first), and keeps the end of its output for the error dialog. It asks for sign-in links and how many conversations have agents at work (each in order; no answer after 5 s). On a stop it asks the server to shut down and kills it if it has not exited in 20 seconds. A stop while the server starts kills it at once instead: it has not listened, so it runs no agent, and quitting while macOS asks for the keychain does not wait for the three minutes. Each server process has its own queues of questions, so one that exits late cannot cancel the next one's. An exit nobody asked for, after the server ran, is reported as a crash.
- **Quitting** (from the menu, on SIGINT or SIGTERM, or when macOS or Linux shuts down) waits for the server to stop before the app exits.
- **The window** shows the chat: a `BrowserWindow` (sandboxed, context isolation, no Node) that loads a sign-in link from the server, so it is signed in at once (as a browser of its own). `guardContents()` (`lifecycle.ts`) keeps its pages on the server, for new windows, navigations and redirects: `linkAction()` sends other web pages and mail to the default browser, opens the server's own pages (files, attachments, which need the window's sign-in) in windows of the app, and goes nowhere for anything else (`file:`, `javascript:`). `permissionAllowed()` lets the server's pages use the microphone (audio only, for voice input), write to the clipboard, go full screen (a video's own button) and show notifications (Settings → Notifications and sound), and nothing else; Electron's permission check for media without a kind counts as one for the microphone (`permissionChecked()`). Its browser data (cookies, localStorage: its sign-in) is in `browser/` of the user-data folder (`profile.ts`, Electron's `sessionData`), which the server is told as `RELAY_APP_BROWSER_DIR` and `protectedReads()` adds to what agents below full access may not read; the rest of the user-data folder holds the data folder, which parallel mode's worktrees are in, so it cannot be kept from them as a whole. A sign-in an earlier build kept in the user-data folder itself is removed at start. **Open Harness Relay** on a window that shows the sign-in page (it has `data-signin`) loads it again with a new link. Downloads use Electron's save dialog. The window's size is kept in `desktop.json`. Closing it leaves the app in the tray (`window-all-closed` is handled); on macOS the Dock icon shows while a window is open (the app is `LSUIElement`). The app menu (Edit, View, Window, in the user's language) gives the window the usual shortcuts, copy and paste included, which macOS only routes through a menu; on Windows and Linux it shows with Alt. **Open in browser** in the tray menu opens the same in the default browser.
- **The tray menu** is rebuilt on every state change, always with the same items (by id), shown or hidden. On macOS and Windows an open menu keeps showing what it showed when it opened: changing an item, or setting a new menu, changes nothing on screen until it opens again (tried with Electron 45 on macOS). So while it is open (`menu-will-show` to `menu-will-close`, and a moment after, since the click on an item comes after the menu closed), its items are changed in place (`patchMenu()`, `traymenu.ts`) and no new menu is set, and `LiveMenu` closes it and opens it again (`closeContextMenu()`, `popUpContextMenu()`; on Windows where the user opened it) when what it shows changed, such as an update's progress, at most once a second. It opens it again 50 ms after closing it (`REOPEN_DELAY_MS`), outside the closing menu's own loop: opened from inside it, each menu runs inside the one before on macOS, and after a few the app takes seconds to quit, or never does. The old menu's close is expected before or after the new one says it opened. Linux's menus (D-Bus) are set anew as before. Opening the app again (`second-instance` on Windows and Linux, `activate` on macOS, the Dock icon) shows the window (`reopenAction()`). Open at login uses Electron's login items on macOS and Windows, and an XDG autostart entry on Linux (`autostart.ts`), started with `--hidden` (macOS reports `wasOpenedAtLogin` instead) so that it does not open the window. On Linux, an autostart entry that is on is written again at each start, so that it starts the AppImage the app runs from.
- **Sharing with the tailnet** runs `tailscale serve --bg`, on HTTPS 443 unless another service, or Funnel, uses that port (then 8443 or 10000: `sharePlan()`, `freeHttpsPort()`; sharing a funneled port for the tailnet would turn Funnel off). When `tailscale serve status --json` cannot be read, nothing is shared.
- **The log** (`log.ts`) is the server's output: started over, the previous one kept as `.old`, past 5 MB, at a start and while the server runs. A log that cannot be written is given up on quietly, never an uncaught error.
- **Updates** (`update.ts`). About 30 seconds after the first start, then every hour (`CHECK_EVERY_MS`; at most every five minutes when the chat asks, `mayAskGitHub()`), a packaged app (unless `RELAY_UPDATES=off`) asks `GET /repos/…/releases?per_page=20` when it is a release candidate or a build of your own (`releases/latest` would leave out pre-releases), and `GET /repos/…/releases/latest` when it is a release (`LATEST_RELEASE_API`: found however many release candidates came out since); `checkNow()` says whether a check asks at all, and a question is given up on after 30 s (`CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`). `parseRelease()` takes a release that is not a draft and whose tag is a version of ours, a release candidate too, with each system's installer by the endings of `site/assets/downloads.js` (`INSTALLERS`, `isInstallerUrl()`), fetched from its copy for updates (`updateCopyUrl()`: `-update` before the extension, `UPDATE_INSTALLERS`, under the same tag in the repository `UPDATES_REPO`, or the one the build named, `RELAY_UPDATES_REPO`, `updateSource.ts`, so that GitHub's download counts tell updates of an installed app from new installs and the release page lists the installers alone; still named as the installer, which the AppImage takes; the installer itself as its `fallback`), a plain file name and GitHub's `digest` (SHA-256) of the installer, the copy being the same file; `newestRelease()` takes the newest by version (`compareSemver()`), not by when it was published, and leaves release candidates out for an app that is a release (`takesCandidates()`: an app whose semver has no `-rc` or `-dev`). When it is newer (`compareSemver()` against `app.getVersion()`) and has this system's installer, the tray menu offers it, and so does the chat: `main.ts` sends the server an `update` message (`DesktopUpdate`: a newer version, an update under way, the last check) each time that changes and once the server listens (`Supervisor.tell()`), the server keeps it (`src/server/updates.ts`, `Updates`) for `GET /api/update`, and passes the chat's asks back (`update-check` with an id, `update-install`, which the supervisor emits as `request`); the app answers each check by its id (`answered`, `CheckAnswers`) in the first state it tells once no check is under way, so a state already on its way never ends a newer check. `installPlan()` says how this copy updates. An app built with the download mirror (`MIRROR`, below in Releasing; `main.ts` passes it as `mirror`) downloads the installer from there first (`downloadUpdate()`, `mirrorAsset()`: under the copy's name, `mirrorCopyUrl()`, which the mirror serves from the installer's file and never counts), in parts as below, checked against the same SHA-256 with nothing to fall back to; only once it has the whole file is GitHub asked as a download from it asks (`countUpdate()`: the copy located, with the token at GitHub's API alone for a private repository, the installer itself when the copy is not there, then the first byte at the CDN it redirects to, without the token), so that the update is counted against the copy as before, once. Any failure on the mirror (not reached, a 404 or 503 for a release it does not have, a release candidate say, a minute without a byte, the wrong file) and the download is made from GitHub as without a mirror, counted there by itself; GitHub not answering the count within 30 s leaves the update uncounted, and downloaded. `download()` writes the installer into `.part` (from its `fallback` when GitHub answers 404 for the copy: a release published before the copies moved, or their repository gone, or private while the app has no token for it) and checks its SHA-256, and gives up after a minute without a byte (`DOWNLOAD_STALL_MS`), so a dropped connection does not hold the update and every check after it. It comes in parts, on up to six connections at once (`DOWNLOAD_CONNECTIONS`), as on some networks GitHub's CDN sends each connection only so fast (some 200 KB/s from Taiwan) and several together go several times faster: GitHub's address is asked once where it leads, without following it (`Locate`; `main.ts` uses `net.request` with `redirect: 'manual'`, as `net.fetch` keeps nothing of a redirect), so that GitHub counts the download once, and the parts are fetched with `Range` from the signed address it sends (good for up to an hour). `main.ts` gives each connection an in-memory session of its own (`updateConnections()`): Chromium sends one session's requests to a host down one HTTP/2 connection, which goes no faster than one request. A connection done with its part takes the second half of what the part with the most left still has, while that is at least twice `MIN_PART_BYTES` (1 MB); a part that fails, or goes a minute without a byte, is asked for again from where it stopped, at a new signed address when its own has expired (403, located once however many parts find it), up to three failures in all (`PART_FAILURES`). A file under 2 MB comes in one request from GitHub's address, and so does one from a server that sends the whole file for a part (from where GitHub sent it). `updateSystem()` picks the installer: the Apple Silicon one on macOS (none on an Intel Mac), the running app's processor's on Windows and Linux (`process.arch`: an x64 app emulated on Windows on Arm stays x64). Once the new version is downloaded and checked, `whenIdle()` waits until the server says no conversation has agents at work (`Supervisor.busy()` every 2 s; one that does not answer is asked again), the menu saying *Update ready: switches once replies finish*, or until the user chooses **Switch to the new version now…** (after a dialog). `busyWithoutAsking()` says what the app knows without asking: a server that stopped by itself (a crash) runs nothing, but one that starts or stops (a restart from the menu) gives no answer yet, and once the app quits (also as the system shuts down) there is never one, so quitting never switches. The running app is not touched until then: after the wait, an app that is quitting puts nothing in place and keeps this version, and the next start removes what was downloaded. On macOS `stageMacApp()` mounts the `.dmg` (`hdiutil`), copies the app next to the running one (`ditto`) and checks it (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`, its bundle id and version) before the wait, and after it `swapMacApp()` renames the two while the server still runs, so that a failed swap changes nothing; a shell started with `spawnDetached()` (`src/server/process.ts`) waits for the app to exit and `open`s the new one. Its script is fixed text: the app's pid and the new version's path reach it in its environment (`HARNESS_RELAY_RELAUNCH_PID`, `HARNESS_RELAY_RELAUNCH_TARGET`, removed before the new version starts), never in its text or its arguments. On Windows the installer runs with `--updated /S --force-run` once the app has quit. On Linux the new AppImage downloads hidden next to the running one (`appImageDownload()`; one left by a quit during the wait is removed at start, `removeAppImageLeftovers()`), is put in place after the wait (`appImageTarget()`, `placeAppImage()`), the autostart entry follows, and it is started once the app has exited. A copy that cannot update itself (`not-packaged`, `no-installer`, `no-checksum`, `read-only`, `not-appimage`) gets the release's page.
- **Updates with a token** (`update.ts`), for an app built with `RELAY_UPDATES_TOKEN`, which a private copies' repository needs (built in, it goes the same way for a public one). `copyToDownload()` first asks GitHub's API for the release under that tag there with the token (`GET /repos/…/releases/tags/<tag>`, given up on after 30 s, `CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, through `lookupSignal`, the installer itself then downloaded), and the copy is downloaded from its address on the API, by its id (`/releases/assets/<id>`), with `downloadHeaders()` (the token, `Accept: application/octet-stream`); where the copy cannot be had (no such release or file; the token expired or revoked, the requests every app shares run out, GitHub's API failing, or any other answer than the file or a redirect to it: 401, 403, 404, 429, 5xx and the rest; no answer at all, also none within 30 s, `answerMs`, as a stalled download is given up on only after a minute, failing the update), the installer itself is downloaded (`unavailable()`, `locateWithToken()`, `answeredWithin()`), also when asked again for an expired signed address halfway through (the parts left then come from the installer, the same file; a part started meanwhile goes on to the copy's address, with the token) and after a server that sends the whole file for a part, so a token gone bad only counts those updates as new installs. Without a token, of GitHub's answers only a 404 sends it to the installer: any other fails the update, which then offers the release's page. A copy that fails its checksum (replaced in its repository, or broken on the way), with or without a token, is fetched once more, from the installer itself and without the token; not when all the bytes came from the installer already (a download that went on from it halfway through is fetched again whole). `main.ts` goes through `downloadUpdate()`, which looks the copy up and downloads it, so that an asset and its headers always go together. The token goes to GitHub's API alone: `download()` refuses headers for an address anywhere else, and asks the copy's address where it leads with them (`Locate` takes the headers, also for a new signed address), the CDN it sends to and the fallback never get it, a request that still carries it (the API answering with the file itself) follows no redirect (`redirect: 'error'`), and without `locate` the fallback is fetched instead. A check never sends it: the releases it reads are this repository's.
- **Its own check.** Started with `--smoke-test[=<report>]`, the app checks itself and exits with 0 or 1, without the single-instance lock and with a temporary user-data folder, so it runs beside a Harness Relay in use: the keyring module loads, the server starts in a utility process (port 0, `RELAY_KEYRING=off`), passes the checks of `selftest.ts`, says no conversation has agents at work, a hidden sandboxed window signs in with a link and shows the chat, and the server stops. With `--smoke-test-clis=<folder>`, the fake CLIs of the end-to-end tests in that folder answer a question first (`checkTurn()`, below): they come first on the server's PATH, with a home folder, CLI homes and a working directory of their own in the temporary folder (`withFakeClis()`), so the user's own CLIs are never started or read. `desktop/scripts/check-package.ts` runs it, always with the fakes.

Building: `npm run desktop` and `npm run desktop:dist` build the UI (`npm run build`), then run `desktop/scripts/build.ts`, which writes `desktop/build/`:

- `app/` holds what Electron runs: `main.cjs`, its `package.json` (from `src/manifest.ts`; `productName` names the user-data folder `Harness Relay`) and the tray icons, and the repository's `LICENSE`, whose terms that `package.json`'s `license` names and which must go with every copy (`electron-builder.yml` also puts it in the app's resources, where people can open it).
- `server/` holds the server, the UI and the keyring module, and what runs apart from the server: Harness Relay's own sandbox host (`relaySandboxHost.mjs`, bundled from `src/server/relaySandboxHost.ts` with the sandbox runtime left out), Pi's gate extension (`piGate.js`, which Pi loads by its path), and the sandbox runtime's packages in `node_modules/` (`@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime` and what it needs; its Linux seccomp helpers are native programs).
- `resources/icon.png` is the app icon, for electron-builder.

`RELAY_VERSION` (set by the [Release workflow](#releasing)) builds the app as a release's version: its semver in `package.json` (see `semverOf()` in `scripts/release.ts`), the version itself in the menu, the log and a problem report (the server's bundle gets it as `__RELAY_VERSION__`, read by `src/server/about.ts`). Without it, the app is a build of the day in Taiwan, with `-dev` and the commit (`devVersion()`): `2026-11-10-dev (30f3d4f)`, as semver `2026.1110.0-dev`, which sorts before every release and release candidate of that day, so that one of that day or later is offered as an update.

`RELAY_UPDATES_REPO` (`owner/name`) names the repository the app downloads its updates from (the installers' copies; the versions and checksums are always this repository's releases), `UPDATES_REPO` (`Agentic-System-Lab/harness-relay-updates`) when it is not set, and `RELAY_UPDATES_TOKEN` a token the app downloads them with on GitHub's API, for a private one (`updateSourceFrom()` in `desktop/src/updateSource.ts`, each trimmed: the build stops on a repository that is not `owner/name` or is this one, and on a token that is not a fine-grained personal access token, `github_pat_` and then letters, digits and `_` (a classic or OAuth token cannot be kept to one repository), and never prints the token). Both go into `main.cjs` alone (not the server's bundle) as `__RELAY_UPDATE_SOURCE__`, fixed for that build: nothing on the computer the app runs on points it elsewhere. The token is encoded there (`encodeUpdateSource()`: base64, reversed; read back by `decodeUpdateSource()` in `main.ts`), so that it never stands in the app as a token's text, which GitHub revokes once it finds one pushed to a public repository or gist; the encoding hides it from no one. The build also writes `build/update-source.txt`, a SHA-256 of the two that names neither (`updateSourceFingerprint()`), which the Release workflow checks against its own. Anyone with a copy of the app can read the token, so let it read that one repository's contents and nothing else; the [Release workflow](#releasing) sets both.

`npm run desktop` then starts Electron on `build/app`. `npm run desktop:dist` runs `desktop/scripts/dist.ts`, which runs electron-builder (`desktop/electron-builder.yml`) and ends by naming the installer and the app to double-click: the installer for the system it runs on (`.dmg`, NSIS `.exe`, `.AppImage`) in `desktop/release/`, and the app itself next to it (`desktop/release/mac-arm64/Harness Relay.app`, say). `npm run desktop:dist -- --output <folder>` puts them elsewhere, to try a build without replacing an app in use. It refuses to start while a Harness Relay runs from the output folder (macOS and Linux, from `ps`; on Windows a running app keeps its files locked anyway), and at the end says when one runs from elsewhere: opening the new build would bring the running one forward instead. Building for another system or processor is not set up: build on each (the Release workflow builds the Arm64 installers on GitHub's Arm runners).

`npx tsx desktop/scripts/smoke.ts` runs the bundled server the way the app does, with plain Node standing in for Electron (`desktop/scripts/parent-port.mjs` gives it a `process.parentPort` over Node's IPC): the keyring module must load from next to the server, the sandbox host and Pi's gate must be next to it and the host must find the sandbox runtime, and the server must listen on 127.0.0.1, serve the UI, refuse the API until a browser signs in, give a sign-in link whose code works, have a team of claude, codex and grok answer a question, print no code, say that no conversation has agents at work, and exit with code 0 when asked to shut down. The team are the end-to-end tests' fake CLIs (`e2e/fake-cli/`, built by `installFakeClis()` from `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts`: scripts on macOS and Linux, Node single executables on Windows): `checkTurn()` in `selftest.ts` starts a read-only roundtable chat of the three, sends it a question and waits for the run to end, and each one's reply must be done and repeat the question, so the server found each CLI by name, spoke its protocol (stream-json, `codex app-server`, ACP) and streamed the reply into the conversation. It never runs the real CLIs. The developer's own `RELAY_*` and `E2E_*` variables are left out of the server's environment (`withoutOwnSettings()` in `selftest.ts`, which the app's own `--smoke-test` and `check-package.ts` use too), and `withFakeClis()` leaves out the user's credentials (API keys and tokens), so a real CLI found instead of a fake has none. CI runs it after building the desktop app, the Release workflow on each system, and `test/parent.test.ts` runs the same checks on `src/server/index.ts`.

`npx tsx desktop/scripts/check-package.ts [--dir <folder>]` checks the packaged app electron-builder left next to the installer (`desktop/release/` by default: `mac-universal/` or `mac-arm64/`, `win-unpacked/` or `win-arm64-unpacked/`, `linux-unpacked/` or `linux-arm64-unpacked/`): its fuses as `electron-builder.yml` sets them (read back with `@electron/fuses`), the server, the web app and the keyring module in its resources (both processors' native parts in a universal Mac app), the sandbox host, Pi's gate and the sandbox runtime (on Linux, its seccomp helper executable), the repository's `LICENSE` in its resources and in its archive (`app.asar`, read with `@electron/asar`), no GitHub token (`github_pat_` and the rest of one) in the files the server runs (`server.mjs`, `relaySandboxHost.mjs`, `piGate.js`: the one for updates is the app's main process's alone) nor as text in the app's `main.cjs` (only encoded), the repository and the token its own environment names (`RELAY_UPDATES_REPO`, `RELAY_UPDATES_TOKEN`; the default repository without them), encoded, in `main.cjs` (it cannot tell what the app was built with, only compare it with these, so run it with the build's values: CI packages the app with a made-up token for this, and the Release workflow gives both the same), and the app's own `--smoke-test`, given the fake CLIs (built first, as for `smoke.ts`) with `--smoke-test-clis`, so the packaged app also has its agents answer a question. The app gets this script's environment without `RELAY_*` and `E2E_*`. On Linux it needs a display (`xvfb-run`) and runs the app with `--no-sandbox`. The Release workflow runs it on each system after making the installer, and CI on a Linux and a Windows package (`electron-builder --linux dir`, `--win dir`).

A few things to know:

- **electron-builder leaves out a `node_modules` folder** at the top of a folder it copies, whatever the filter says. So the keyring module is copied as an entry of its own in `extraResources`.
- **Fuses.** The packaged app turns off Electron's run-as-Node mode and `NODE_OPTIONS` / `--inspect` (`electronFuses`). Otherwise any program could run code as the app and use the privacy permissions macOS gave it. This is also why the shell's environment is read with `env -0` and not by running the app as Node, and why Harness Relay's own sandbox host, a Node program run for each sandboxed open-agent turn, runs in the app with the Node.js 22.12 or later found on the login shell's `PATH` (`hostNode()` in `relaySandbox.ts`): without one, the open agents are refused below full access with a message saying so (`relaySandboxNoNode`), and full access still works. It loads its code only from its archive, and checks the archive (`enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation`, on macOS and Windows), and `file:` pages get no extra privileges (`grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges: false`; the window shows only the server's pages). Cookie encryption stays off: the window's sign-in is in a folder agents below full access may not read, its key is in localStorage, which it would not encrypt, and on an app signed ad hoc macOS would ask for the keychain after every update. `check-package.ts` reads all of them back.
- **Signing.** On macOS the app is signed ad hoc (`mac.identity: '-'`, and `dist.ts` turns off electron-builder's search of the keychain), on every computer the same: never with a certificate electron-builder would otherwise find in your keychain. Its entitlements (`desktop/entitlements.mac.plist`) are electron-builder's defaults plus the microphone, which the hardened runtime refuses otherwise; Info.plist says why it asks (`NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`, translated in `desktop/macos/*.lproj/InfoPlist.strings`). Apple Silicon runs no unsigned code, and the fuses change the Electron binary after it was linked: an unsigned build is killed at launch (exit code 137). The ad-hoc signature is not trusted by Gatekeeper, so a downloaded app needs **Open Anyway**, and macOS asks again for the keychain after each update. Signing with a Developer ID and notarizing, and signing for Windows, are not set up yet. Updates are the app's own (`update.ts`), not electron-updater's, and rest on GitHub's checksums over HTTPS.
- **The Mac app is for Apple Silicon only** in a release (`--mac dmg --arm64`, the macOS runner's own processor): there is no app for Intel Macs, which run Harness Relay from the source. The website counts a Mac as one with Apple Silicon unless Chromium's hints say Intel (Safari and Firefox say "Intel" on every Mac); the app offers no update on an Intel Mac. The Mac apps of 2026-11-11 and its release candidate were universal and look for `-mac-universal.dmg`, so they do not find the releases after them. `npm run desktop:dist` builds for the Mac it runs on.
- **Windows and Linux have an installer per processor** in a release: x64 (`win-x64.exe`, `linux-x86_64.AppImage`) and Arm64 (`win-arm64.exe`, `linux-arm64.AppImage`). The Arm64 ones are built on GitHub's Arm runners (`windows-11-arm`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, free for public repositories), not cross-built, so that npm installs the keyring module's native part for Arm and the packaged app's own check runs there. The Windows Arm64 job builds on a Dev Drive of its own (a copy of the checkout, npm's cache and the temporary folder): the runner's one disk writes small files about 14 times slower than a Dev Drive does, which made `npm ci` take two minutes there.
- **Linux AppImages** may need `--no-sandbox` where unprivileged user namespaces are restricted (Ubuntu 24.04). electron-builder's `executableArgs` only reach a `.desktop` file, not an AppImage started directly; the app passes the flag on to its autostart entry when it was started with it.
- **To try it apart from your own data**, run the built app with a user-data folder of its own and without opening its window, and give that folder a `desktop.json` with another port if a Harness Relay already listens on 8787: `desktop/node_modules/.bin/electron desktop/build/app --user-data-dir="$HOME/relay-desktop-try" --hidden` (not under `/tmp`: the server warns about a data folder in a temporary folder, which sandboxes let agents write in). The server's log is `~/Library/Logs/Harness Relay/server.log` on macOS, and `logs/server.log` in the user-data folder on Windows and Linux.

## Request flow

1. The client posts `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages` → `Orchestrator.send()` prepares the workspace (for edit / full access: `git init` if needed, and the directory lock unless the mode is parallel), appends the user message and starts `execute()` in the background (the request returns `202`).
2. In an auto-team conversation, a run for a new question only lets the lead agent design the team (`designTeam()`, see [Architecture → Auto team](architecture.md#auto-team)); the team answers in a run of its own once the design is confirmed. Otherwise `execute()` chooses speakers by mode (creating per-agent worktrees first in parallel mode) and calls `turn()` for each.
3. `turn()` creates a streaming agent message, records the starting state (worktree `HEAD` or a snapshot), computes the messages the agent hasn't seen, builds the prompt (`prompt.ts`), and calls `runners[provider].run()` in the right directory, passing `requestApproval` unless the conversation refuses automatically.
4. The adapter spawns the CLI (`process.ts`; an open agent's through `startOpenProcess()`, inside the relay sandbox below full access), parses each stdout line and emits `AgentEvent`s.
5. `turn()` applies events to the message (text, thinking, plan, tool rows and output), emits SSE events through the `Hub`, and saves through the `Store`. An approval request adds an approval part and waits for `POST …/approvals/:approvalId`. When the CLI exits it records the files changed (commit in the worktree, or a second snapshot) on the message.

## HTTP API

All write requests must send `Content-Type: application/json` (CSRF guard). Errors are `{ "error": string }` with status 400/404/409/415/500. Send `X-Relay-Lang: zh-TW | en` to get errors, system notes and built-in templates in that language (the web app always does).

Every request but `GET /api/auth` and `POST /api/auth/login` must come from a signed-in browser ([Architecture → Signing in](architecture.md#signing-in)): the `relay_session` cookie and the `X-Relay-Key` header. The routes the browser loads by itself (`BROWSER_LOADS` in `app.ts`: replies' media and downloads, attachments, `files/raw`, `files/download`, `files/zip`) take the cookie alone, and `…/events` takes the key as `?key=`. Otherwise the answer is 401 with `code: "signed-out"`. Tests that are not about signing in pass `auth: null` to `createApp()`.

| Method & path | Body → response |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/auth` | → `{ signedIn, desktop? }`: whether the request comes from a signed-in browser (always true without sign-in); `desktop: true` when the desktop app started the server (the sign-in page then points at its menu) |
| `POST /api/auth/login` | `{ code }` → `{ key }`, and sets the `relay_session` cookie (replacing the session of a browser signed in already); 400 for a wrong or expired code, 429 after ten wrong codes in a minute |
| `POST /api/auth/codes` | → `{ code, expiresAt }`: a one-time code for another device, working for ten minutes |
| `GET /api/auth/sessions` | → `AuthSessionView[]`: the signed-in browsers, the latest used first, `current` for the one asking |
| `DELETE /api/auth/sessions/:id` | signs that browser out (and clears this one's cookie when it is this one); 404 for an unknown one |
| `GET /api/conversations` | → `ConversationSummary[]` (with `running`, `waiting`, `lastStatus`, `lastReplyId`, `groupId`, `sorting`, `createdAt` and `branchFrom` for the sidebar). `?page=<id>`: the page asking (its own id, up to 64 letters, digits, `-` and `_`), with `&visible=1` while it is in front, so that the server holds its browser's push notifications, and without it when it is not (a tab in the background, a page that came back), so that the server sends what it held ([Architecture → Notifications](architecture.md#notifications)) |
| `GET /api/push` | → `PushStatus`: `{ publicKey }`, the key a browser subscribes to push notifications with (VAPID, base64url; made the first time it is asked), or `{ error }` when the server cannot send them (its key could not be saved in the database) |
| `PUT /api/push/subscription` | `{ subscription: { endpoint, keys: { p256dh, auth } }, prefs: { done, needs, sound } }` → `{ ok }`: keeps this browser's push subscription, for its signed-in browser and in the request's language (updating its own one of the same `endpoint`). 409 with `code: "push-taken"` when another signed-in browser has that subscription, and with `code: "push-gone"` when its push service said it is gone (either way the browser subscribes anew); a browser keeps its latest five; 400 for an address that is not a known push service's over HTTPS (`PUSH_HOSTS` in `src/server/push.ts`), keys that are not a P-256 public key and a 16-byte secret, or preferences that are not booleans; 503 when the server cannot send push notifications |
| `DELETE /api/push/subscription` | `{ endpoint }` → `{ ok }`: removes this browser's subscription (only its own) |
| `POST /api/push/test` | `{ endpoint }` → `{ ok }`: sends this browser a test notification through its push service; 404 when that subscription is not this browser's, 502 when the push service did not take it (a subscription it says is gone is removed) |
| `POST /api/push/presence` | `{ visible, page }` → `{ ok }`: this browser's page (`page`: its id) went to the background (`false`: the push notifications held for the browser are sent now, unless another of its pages is still in front) |
| `GET /api/search?q=` | → `SearchResult[]`: conversations whose title or user / agent messages contain every word of `q` (ignoring case), title matches first, then the most recent; at most 50. A branch's result has `branchFrom`, the title of the conversation it was branched from |
