| `GET /api/cwds` | → `WorkingDirectories`: `{ home, dirs: [{ path, default? }] }`, the working directories the working directories box offers: the default ones (`default: true`, `~` expanded as a new conversation reads them) first, then the conversations' own (all of each), the most recently used first; each folder once (ignoring case on macOS and Windows), and only folders that still exist. `home` lets a typed `~` match. `pick: true` when the browser can open the folder dialog: it asks by a loopback name (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, never through `tailscale serve`) and this computer has one |
| `POST /api/cwds/pick` | `{ start? }` → `PickedFolders`: `{ paths }`, the folders chosen in this computer's own folder dialog (several on macOS and with zenity; one with kdialog and on Windows), none when it was cancelled. It opens at `start` (`~` expanded), or the nearest folder above it that exists, or the default working directory when `start` is empty, and is answered when the dialog closes (closed when the request is, or after ten minutes). 403 when not asked by a loopback name, 503 when this computer has no dialog (Linux without zenity or kdialog, or without a graphical session), 409 while one is open already, 502 when it could not be opened |
| `PUT /api/conversations/:id/group` | `{ groupId }` → `{ ok }`: puts the conversation in a sidebar group (`null` = Other), and with it the rest of its family of branched conversations; 404 for an unknown group. A conversation the user moved is never sorted automatically |
| `GET /api/groups` | → `ConversationGroup[]`, in the order they were made (the default group, Other, is not listed) |
| `POST /api/groups` | `{ name }` → `ConversationGroup` (400 for an empty, too long or taken name — including Other / 其他 — or more than 50 groups) |
| `PATCH /api/groups/:id` | `{ name }` → `ConversationGroup` |
| `DELETE /api/groups/:id` | deletes the group; its conversations go to Other |
| `POST /api/conversations` | `{ title?, agents, settings, createCwd? }` → `Conversation`; with `settings.autoTeam: true`, `agents` is ignored (empty) — the lead agent designs the team for each new question, and `settings.autoConfirm: true` uses its team without asking; `settings.extraCwds`: the working directories after `settings.cwd` (at most eight in all, each once; without `settings.cwd`, the default ones); working directories that do not exist are a 404 with `code: "cwd-missing"` and `paths` (those missing) unless `createCwd: true`, which creates them |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id` | → `Conversation` |
| `PATCH /api/conversations/:id` | `{ title?, agents?, settings?, createCwd? }` → `Conversation` (409 while running; neither `agents` nor `settings` switches auto team on or off, `settings.autoConfirm` can change; setting `agents` confirms a team design that waits, and that team then answers; `settings` without a `cwd` keeps the conversation's working directories, and a `cwd` without `extraCwds` makes it the only one; missing working directories as in `POST`) |
| `DELETE /api/conversations/:id` | stops a run and deletes |
| `PUT /api/conversations/:id/schedule` | `{ text, repeat, timeZone?, enabled?, approvals?, missed?, newChat? }` → `ConversationSchedule`: sets the conversation's schedule (one per conversation; replaces the one there was, keeping how it went last time). `repeat` is `{ kind: 'once', at }`, `{ kind: 'daily', time }`, `{ kind: 'weekly', time, days }` (0 = Sunday) or `{ kind: 'interval', hours }` (1–168, from when its times were last set; saving only a new message or other choices keeps the cadence); `time` is `HH:MM` in `timeZone` (default: the server's). Defaults: on, `approvals: 'deny'`, `missed: 'skip'`, `newChat: false` (`true`: each time in a new conversation with the same team, settings and group). 400 for an empty or too long message, a bad time, no days, a one-time schedule in the past (when on) or an unknown time zone; 409 when it is on and the team is still to be designed or confirmed |
| `POST /api/schedules` | `{ title?, settings, createCwd?, schedule }` → `Conversation`: a new schedule (**New schedule**) in a conversation of its own, with auto team (`settings` as in `POST /api/conversations`; `schedule` as in `PUT …/schedule`, on by default; the title defaults to the message's first line). The lead agent starts designing the team for the schedule's message at once (`designForSchedule`); nothing is posted, and once the team is confirmed nothing runs until the schedule sends the message. The schedule is checked before the working directory is created; 400 for a schedule that could never send, 404 (`code: CWD_MISSING`) for a missing working directory without `createCwd` |
| `DELETE /api/conversations/:id/schedule` | → `{ ok }`: removes the schedule |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/schedule/design` | → `202` `{ ok }`: has the lead agent design the team for the schedule's message (again, e.g. after a design was stopped), as `POST /api/schedules` does. 404 without a schedule; 400 when the conversation has no auto team; 409 when it has a team already, while it runs, or while a design waits for confirmation |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/schedule/run` | → `202` `{ ok, queued, chatId? }`: sends the schedule's message now, as when it comes due (it waits in the queue while the team replies; `chatId`: the new conversation it opened, for a schedule with `newChat`); its times stay as they are. 404 without a schedule; 409 with the reason when it is held back (the team waits for an answer, is not confirmed, or an earlier scheduled message is still waiting), the conversation is busy (a settings change, an apply, another conversation writing in the same folder) or it cannot start |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages` | `{ text, attachments?, interrupt? }` (empty text and no files = continue) → `202` `{ ok, queued }`. While the team replies (or its design waits for confirmation) the message waits in the queue (`queued: true`) and is delivered before the next turn; `interrupt: true` stops the team and sends it now. `attachments`: `[{ name, mimeType, data }]` with base64 `data`; at most 10 files of 20 MB each |
| `DELETE /api/conversations/:id/queue/:messageId` | → `ChatMessage`: withdraws a message still waiting to be delivered (404 once it was delivered) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/attachments/:attachmentId` | → the attached file (images inline, anything else as a download) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/media?path=` | → an image or video the reply embeds, from the working directory, the agent's worktree or the attachments (`nosniff`, sandboxed, byte ranges, ETag; anything else is a 404) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/download?path=` | → a file the reply links to, from the same folders (never `.git`), always as a download (`application/octet-stream`, `attachment`, `nosniff`, sandboxed, byte ranges with `If-Range`, ETag; HEAD sends no body) |
| `PUT /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId` | `{ text }` → `202`: edit a user message — it and everything after it are deleted, every agent starts a fresh session, and the team answers again (`Orchestrator.resend()`) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/retry` | → `202`: delete an agent reply and what follows, and let that agent (and the later speakers of its round) answer again |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/branch` | → the new conversation: a copy of the messages up to and including an agent reply, with the team, settings, "always allow" answers and sidebar group, which goes on from there on its own (`Orchestrator.branch()`; 400 for a message that is not an agent's reply, 409 while a message before it is still streaming) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/approvals/:approvalId` | `{ allow, always?, note? }`: answer an agent's approval request (404 when already answered or expired) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/confirm-plan` | confirms the lead agent's team design that waits: its team becomes the conversation's and answers the question it was for, with the messages that waited (409 while something else holds the conversation) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/decline-plan` | declines it: the current team stays and answers (400 for a first question, which has no team to keep; 409 as above) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/save-plan` | `{ name? }` → `TeamPreset`: saves the lead agent's custom team design as a template |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/revise-team` | `{ request, agents, settings }` (`settings`: `mode`, `rounds`, `maxHops`, and the drawer's `permission` and `cwd` for the lead to read) → `TeamRevision` (`agents`, `settings`, `reason`, `meta`): the lead agent changes the team sent (the team drawer's) as asked, read-only; nothing is saved (404 for an unknown conversation; 400 for an empty or too long request, or an invalid team; 503 when the lead's CLI fails, its reply is unusable, or the server is shutting down; 409 when it was stopped: the request closed, a newer one came, or the conversation was deleted) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/stop` | aborts the run |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/share` | `{ html, upTo?, files? }` → `SharedPage`: puts the page the UI made (`sharePage.tsx`) on the web as a secret gist with the user's `gh` (`gh gist create`), read at `url` (gistpreview.github.io), and records it; `upTo`: the agent reply it ends with. `files`: `ShareFileRef[]` (`{ messageId, path }`, at most 100), the links the page's placeholder links (`relay-share-file:<n>`) name: those files, found as `…/download` would serve them, go into a second secret gist first (`gh api gists --method POST` with a placeholder, its description holding the share's token, and recorded at once as an unfinished share; then git pushes the files into it, signed in by `gh`, and `gh api gists/<id>` reads back their raw URLs), then the page gist is made with each placeholder replaced by its file's raw URL; `files` and `filesGist` in the answer say how many and where. 400 for an empty page or a list of files that is not one (or a placeholder it does not cover), 413 over `SHARE_MAX_BYTES` (1 MB, with 512 bytes for each file link), more than 20 files or 100 MB of them, 409 with `code: share-file-refused` for a file that cannot go along (any `ShareFileProblem`), 502 with the reason when `gh` is missing, not signed in or fails, or git cannot push the files (nothing recorded, except a files gist left behind when neither the page nor deleting it went through: recorded with `unfinished`, its `id` and `gistUrl` the files gist's, `url` empty, and no gh `code`), with `code: gh-missing` or `gh-signed-out` for the first two (the UI shows the steps to fix them) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/share/files` | `{ files, upTo? }` → `{ files: ShareFileInfo[] }`: the files these links of the shared messages (up to and including `upTo`) point to, each once, with the links that point to it (`refs`), its `size`, and a `problem` when it cannot go into the gist (`missing`, `outside` the chat's folders or in `.git`, `too-large` over 50 MB); found only as `…/download` would serve them, measured but not read, and nothing of what they hold is sent. 400 for a list that is not one |
| `GET /api/shares` | → `SharedPage[]`, the newest first; `conversationGone` once its conversation was deleted, `files` and `filesGist` when files went along, `unfinished` for a files gist left without its page (not while its share is still being made) |
| `DELETE /api/shares/:id` | → `{ ok }`: deletes the share's gists, the files gist first, then the page's (`gh gist delete --yes`; one GitHub cannot find either, by `gh api gists/:id`, counts as deleted, while one of another account is refused), then the record, only once both are gone. 404 for an unknown share, 409 for the files gist of a share still being made, 502 with the reason (and the same codes) when `gh` fails for either (the record stays, to be removed again) |
| `POST /api/gh/login` | `{}` → `CliLoginView`: starts gh's own sign-in for sharing (`gh auth login --web --hostname github.com`, in gh's environment for sharing, `ghEnv()`), stopping one already running; GitHub's one-time code and device page come in `codes` and `urls` as gh prints them |
| `GET /api/gh/login` | → `CliLoginView` or `null`: that sign-in as it runs (`running`, then `success`, `failed` or `cancelled`) |
| `DELETE /api/gh/login` | → `{ ok }`: stops it |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files?root=&path=` | → `FileListing`: the folders that can be browsed (`roots`: `cwd`, `cwd:1`… for the other working directories, plus each agent's worktree of each in parallel mode, `<agent>`, `<agent>:1`…), and the entries of `path` in `root` (folders first; `.git` and links leading outside left out; at most 2,000). 404 for anything outside the folder |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/preview?root=&path=` | → `FilePreview`: the text of a file (the first 512 KB), or that it is an image, a video or binary |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/raw?root=&path=` | → an image or video in the folder, served like the media endpoint (anything else is a 404) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/download?root=&path=` | → any file in the folder as a download, like the message download endpoint (a folder, or anything outside or in `.git`, is a 404) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/zip/check?root=&path=` | → `ZipCheck` `{ all, unignored? }`: what downloading the folder (`path` '' = the root) as a ZIP file would hold, `{ files, bytes }`, or null when it is more than 10,000 files and folders or 1 GB; when `all` is null, `unignored` is the same for only what git does not ignore (null when that is too much too, or the folder is not in a repository). Nothing is read or zipped; 404 as for listing |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/files/zip?root=&path=&unignored=` | → the folder as a ZIP file, zipped as it is sent (`application/zip`, attachment, `nosniff`, sandboxed), its entries in a folder of its name; the same entries as the browser lists (no `.git`, links only to what is inside, each folder once, empty folders kept), with their permission bits and names safe for any unzip tool; an agent's worktree root is named `<working directory>-<agent>`; `unignored=1`: only the files git does not ignore (in repositories inside it too). 413 when it is too large; 404 as for listing; `HEAD` gives the headers only |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/messages/:messageId/diff` | → unified diff (text) of that turn's changes (in several repositories: one after another, each one's paths starting with its folder's name) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/agents/:agentId/diff` | → unified diff (text) of an agent's whole worktree result (all its worktrees, named so) |
| `POST /api/conversations/:id/agents/:agentId/apply` | applies an agent's worktree result to the working directories → `{ files }` (all its worktrees, every patch checked first: 409 on a conflict in any, and nothing is applied) |
| `GET /api/conversations/:id/events` | SSE stream of `ServerEvent` |
| `GET /api/presets` · `POST /api/presets` · `DELETE /api/presets/:id` | team templates (built-in ones first); `POST` takes `{ id?, name, description?, agents, settings }`, and the id of a saved template (`user-…`) updates it |
| `GET /api/settings` · `PUT /api/settings` | `{ defaultCwd?, defaultExtraCwds?, createCwd?, lead?, agentHome?, agentsViewUrl?, agentsViewLink? }` (`lead`: `{ provider, model?, effort?, account? }`, the auto-team lead agent, `account` one of that CLI's account ids or left out for the default one; `defaultExtraCwds`: the default working directories after `defaultCwd`, given with it (none when left out), at most eight in all, each once; ones that do not exist are a 404 with `code: "cwd-missing"` and `paths` unless `createCwd: true`, which creates them; `agentHome`: the folder of the CLIs' own homes, an absolute or `~` path, created when missing, not the home folder, a root, or inside Harness Relay's folder or a default working directory, `""` = off, 409 while a conversation runs, is sorted into a group or has its team changed by the lead agent; `agentsViewUrl`: AgentsView's address, `http(s)` on `localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `[::1]` only, saved as its origin, `""` = off; `agentsViewLink`: where the UI's **Open AgentsView** goes instead, any http(s) address, `""` = `agentsViewUrl`). The answer has `agentsViewError` while the last update of AgentsView at the current address failed |
| `PUT /api/settings/providers/:provider` | `{ bin?, apiKey?, sandboxTerminal? }` → `SettingsView`: what the CLI has once for all its accounts (its executable), and the default account's key (`apiKey: null` removes). A request that names `defaultModel`, `defaultEffort`, `nativeOff` or `nativeItems` is a 400: those are each account's (below). `sandboxTerminal`: whether OpenHands' commands may use a terminal in the relay sandbox on macOS, ignored for the other CLIs ([Open agents → OpenHands](open-agents.md#openhands)); an open agent has no key here, its keys are the model backends'. |
| `GET /api/backends` | → `ModelBackendsView`: the open agents' model backends, `apis` (`openai`, `anthropic`, `xai`) and `endpoints`, keys masked, with where each is kept (`keyStorage`) and `keyUnreadable` for one the credential store could not give at startup, and `deletedEndpoints`: the ids of endpoints deleted and neither added again nor forgotten |
| `PUT /api/backends/:id` | `{ apiKey }` → `ModelBackendsView`: sets an API backend's key in the credential store (`backend-<id>-api-key`); `null` or `""` removes it. 404 for another id |
| `PUT /api/backends/endpoints/:id` | `{ name, baseUrl, models, apiKey? }` → `ModelBackendsView`: adds a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint, or replaces the one with this id (a lowercase slug, not `openai` / `anthropic` / `xai`; it cannot change). `baseUrl`: http(s) without credentials, query or fragment, not Harness Relay's own address; `models`: the model names it serves, without spaces (at most 100); `apiKey` absent keeps its key, `null` removes it (`endpoint-<id>-api-key`). Adding one under a deleted id takes that id out of `deletedEndpoints`, in the same write. 400 for what does not fit or more than 20 endpoints |
| `DELETE /api/backends/endpoints/:id` | → `ModelBackendsView`: deletes the endpoint and its key, and remembers its id (`deleted_endpoints`): a Claude Code, Codex, Grok or Copilot model `<id>/…` then fails its turn instead of reaching the CLI's own service; 404 for an unknown one |
| `DELETE /api/backends/deleted-endpoints/:id` | → `ModelBackendsView`: forgets a deleted endpoint's id, so that models `<id>/…` go to their CLI as they are again (a model of the user's own Codex config that starts with that name, say); 404 for an id not remembered, 400 for one that is not an endpoint id |
| `POST /api/settings/providers/:provider/accounts` | `{ label }` → `{ id, settings: SettingsView }`: adds an account to the CLI, signed out and with no key (`label`: at most 40 characters on one line; empty is allowed); a Grok account starts with a copy of the default account's folder trust. 409 at the account limit (`RELAY_MAX_ACCOUNTS`, else 10, the default one included); 400 for an open agent, which has no sign-in, so no other account |
| `PUT /api/settings/providers/:provider/accounts/:account` | `{ label?, apiKey?, defaultModel?, defaultEffort?, nativeOff?, nativeItems? }` → `SettingsView`: renames the account (`default` = the default one), sets its key (`null` removes it; a Codex key signs that account in with `codex login --with-api-key` and is not kept), or changes how Harness Relay runs it: its default model and effort (empty = the CLI's own, from its home), `nativeOff`: the whole list of the CLI's own parts its runs leave out, of `skills`, `mcp`, `tools`, `instructions`, `memory`, `hooks`, `agents`, `plugins` (parts the CLI cannot leave out are dropped); `nativeItems`: all the single items its runs leave out, `{ <kind>: [id…] }` with kinds `skills`, `mcp`, `mcpTools`, `tools`, `instructions`, `hooks`, `agents`, `plugins` and the ids from `NativeSetup`, at most 2,000 per kind (kinds the CLI cannot leave out alone are dropped). Each account in `SettingsView.providers[].accounts` has these four. 404 for an account the CLI does not have |
| `DELETE /api/settings/providers/:provider/accounts/:account` | → `SettingsView`: forgets the account and its key (agents that named it, and the lead agent, use the default account from then on), then signs it out (as far as the CLI can in 15 s) and deletes its folder. 400 for `default`, 409 while a conversation runs |
| `GET /api/tools` | → `ToolLibraryView`: MCP servers (variable and header values masked), skills (read from their `SKILL.md` now, with a `problem` when one cannot be used), memory per scope, where secrets are kept |
| `PUT /api/tools` | `{ skills?, memory? }` → `ToolLibraryView`. `skills` replaces the list (`[{ path, providers? }]`; a new path may be a folder of skills, and must hold at least one); `memory` changes the given parts (`all` or a CLI's id: `claude`, `codex`, `grok`, `cursor`, `antigravity`, `copilot`, `muse`, `opencode`, `goose`, `cline`, `openhands`, `pi`; at most 20,000 characters each) |
| `PUT /api/tools/mcp/:name` | `{ transport, command?, args?, url?, env?, headers?, providers?, previousName? }` → `ToolLibraryView`: adds a server, or with `previousName` replaces or renames one. A variable or header without `value` keeps its stored value. 409 for a taken name |
| `DELETE /api/tools/mcp/:name` | → `ToolLibraryView` (its values are removed from the credential store) |
| `GET /api/tools/skills/scan?path=` | → `{ path, skills: SkillCandidate[] }`: the skills under a folder (the folder itself and skill folders up to two levels down, at most 300), marked when already added; nothing is saved |
| `GET /api/providers[?refresh=1]` | → `ProviderStatus[]`, each with `accounts`: every account's sign-in, the default one first; `checkedAt`: when its check began (a cached answer keeps its check's) |
| `GET /api/providers/:provider/usage[?account=<id>][&refresh=1]` | → `AccountUsage`: how much of the account's plan is used (the default account's without `account`; 404 for one the CLI does not have), as its CLI reports it without a prompt, in that account's home: its plan, each limit (`span` or `minutes`; `kind` for what Copilot counts, or `scope` for a model's own or the CLI's own name; `usedPercent`, `resetsAt`; `used` and `total` for a limit of requests), the `credits` left (Codex's, Antigravity's); or `none` and why (`apiKey`: an account on a key its provider bills by use, told without asking its CLI except Claude Code and Codex, which say so themselves; `signedOut`; `noAllowance`; `unlimited`; `noData`; `unavailable`: Claude Code without plan limits for how the account signs in (a token from `claude setup-token`, Bedrock, Vertex); `notReported`: Cursor, which shows its usage only in its own terminal UI and gives its plan from `about` (not for an account on a key, nor another account, whose plan `about` would not describe), and an open agent); or `error` in the CLI's words, or `problem` in Harness Relay's (`timedOut`, `noAnswer`, `cliMissing`: a code the UI words, as the answer is kept for every browser whatever its language). Each CLI is asked in one empty folder, the same each time (`data/tmp/usage-probe`). Claude Code answers a `get_usage` control request (in an empty folder, without settings files, hooks or MCP servers), Codex `account/read` and `account/rateLimits/read` on `codex app-server`, Grok `_x.ai/billing` over ACP (without a session, its own setup left out), Muse Code `muse usage --json`, Copilot `account.getQuota` and `account.getCurrentAuth` on its headless server (`copilot --headless --stdio`, `Content-Length` framing; in a home of the question's own with a copy of the account's sign-in state, and a fresh cache folder), Antigravity `/usage` and then `/credits` in print mode (`agy -p /usage --output-format json`; stopped as soon as it asks for a sign-in on stderr). Kept two minutes per account; dropped, and an ask under way stopped, when the CLI's cached state is (a sign-in, key or setting changed here), when a status check finds an account signed in otherwise than the last one did, and with a removed account; `refresh=1` asks again unless an ask is under way |
| `GET /api/limits` | → `ServerLimits` `{ maxAccounts, maxTeamAgents, turnTimeoutMinutes }`: what the environment sets that the UI keeps to or shows (`RELAY_MAX_ACCOUNTS`, `RELAY_MAX_TEAM_AGENTS`, `RELAY_TURN_TIMEOUT_MIN`) |
| `GET /api/about` | → `AboutInfo` `{ version, desktop, os, node }`: which Harness Relay this is (the version the desktop app was built as, else `source (<commit>)`) and on what, for a problem report; no paths or names |
| `GET /api/update` | → `UpdateStatus` `{ current, updates, available?, progress?, checking?, checkedAt?, checkFailed? }`: this version, and what the desktop app last said of its updates (`updates: false`, with nothing else, for `npm start`) |
| `POST /api/update/check` | Has the desktop app ask GitHub now (at most every five minutes; otherwise what it knows) → `UpdateStatus` once the app has answered this check by its id (or after 35 s, longer than the app gives GitHub) |
| `POST /api/update/install` | Has the desktop app update to the newer version, as from its menu (it asks the user first, in its own dialog) → `{ ok: true }`; 409 when there is none, or one is under way |
| `POST /api/report/screenshots` | `{ files: [{ data }] }` (base64, at most 5, each at most 10 MB; PNG, JPEG or WebP by their bytes, not GIF) → `ReportScreenshots` `{ id, url }`: a problem report's screenshots as one secret gist through the user's `gh`, named `screenshot-1.png`… (never their own names) with only what showing them needs (`withoutMetadata()`: no location, EXIF but the orientation, XMP, IPTC, comments, thumbnails, text, content credentials or anything after the image; a JPEG or PNG cut short after its image data began ended there, a WebP cut short refused; 400 for one it cannot read through), `url` its page on GitHub for the mail; 400 for none or one that is not an image, 413 for too many or too large, 502 for `gh`'s trouble (`code`: `gh-missing`, `gh-signed-out`) |
| `DELETE /api/report/screenshots/:id` | Deletes that gist again: only one uploaded since the server started (404 otherwise) |
| `GET /api/models[?refresh=1]` | → `ProviderModels[]`: one per account of each CLI, asked in its home (its CLI defaults are from its own config) and with its own defaults in `defaultModel` / `defaultEffort`; each CLI's default account first, with no `account`, the others with theirs (`catalogFor()` picks one). Kept for ten minutes per account; changing an account's defaults shows at once |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/native[?account=<id>]` | `{ refresh? }` → `NativeSetup`: the skills, MCP servers (names, kinds and states only) and their tools, built-in tools (Claude Code), hooks (never their commands), subagents and plugins (when the CLI lists them) and instruction and memory files the CLI loads by itself for the default working directory, in the home of the default account or of `account` (404 for one the CLI does not have), with `errors` for what it did not report. Each item the CLI can leave out alone has the `id` it is left out by. With anything left out of that account's runs, the CLI is asked again the way Harness Relay starts it as that account: `nativeOff`, `nativeItems` and `asRun` say so, and items it then leaves out have `leftOut: true`. Starts the CLI briefly without a prompt (a POST, so other sites cannot set it off; Claude Code gets a `/usage` turn it answers by itself; Cursor is not started, its lists come from the files it reads; Antigravity answers `agy -p /skills`, `/hooks` and `/agents` and `agy plugin list --json`, its MCP servers and instruction files come from the files it reads; Copilot answers `copilot mcp list --json`, `skill list --json`, `instruction list --json` and `plugin list --json`, its hooks and custom agents come from the files it reads; Muse Code answers `muse skills list --json` in a config folder of the question's own, as a read-only run would, so a project's skills, which it loads only in a workspace it trusts, are left out, and its MCP servers and hooks come from its settings; an open agent is asked through its adapter's own probe, or answers that Harness Relay cannot list what it loads yet); kept for a minute per CLI, executable, folder and account while what that account leaves out stays the same (`refresh: true` asks again) |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/native/file[?account=<id>]` | `{ path }` → `FilePreview`: one of the files the CLI reported for that account (an instruction or memory file, or a skill's `SKILL.md`, matched exactly), to read in the UI; any other path is a 404. Checked against that account's latest answer however old, so opening a file does not start the CLI again |
| `POST /api/providers/grok/folder-trust` | `{ cwd?, trust?, folder? }` → `GrokFolderTrust` `{ cwd, folder, above, trusted }`: whether Grok trusts `cwd` (default: the default working directory), from `grok inspect`. `folder` is the one Grok decides trust for (the repository root, links resolved, else `cwd`'s real path); `above` says it is a repository root above `cwd`. With `trust: true` (sent only after the user confirmed `folder`) it asks Grok again and records the trust for that folder as `grok --trust` would, in `$GROK_HOME/trusted_folders.toml` of every Grok account (all or none); 409 when Grok now names another folder than the `folder` sent, or still reports it untrusted (the file is then put back as it was) |
| `GET /api/providers/:provider/login-methods` | → `LoginMethod[]` (empty for an open agent) |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/login[?account=]` | `{ method }` → `LoginSessionView`. Here and in the next four routes, `account` is one of the CLI's account ids (none, or `default`, = the default account; 404 for one it does not have), and each account has its own sign-in. 400 for an open agent, which has no sign-in, for a Cursor, Antigravity or Muse Code account besides the default one, which signs in with its API key only (`keyOnlyAccount()`), and on Windows for a CLI that is a script Harness Relay cannot start (`WindowsScriptError`) |
| `GET /api/providers/:provider/login[?account=]` | → `LoginSessionView \| null` (poll while running) |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/login/input[?account=]` | `{ text }` → writes to the login process's stdin |
| `DELETE /api/providers/:provider/login[?account=]` | cancels the login |
| `POST /api/providers/:provider/logout[?account=]` | signs the account out (400 for an open agent; a Cursor, Antigravity or Muse Code account besides the default one kept no sign-in, so nothing runs; Antigravity's default account signs out with agy's ACP `logout` method, in its home; each Copilot account with Copilot's ACP `logout` method, in its home; Muse Code's runs `muse logout` in its folders, which drops the key and the Meta sign-in muse keeps, not a `META_API_KEY`; Cursor's default account runs `cursor-agent logout` without its key, in the credential store the server was started with (`AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE`) if it names one, which signs out of Cursor's own sign-in) |
| `GET /api/voice` | → `VoiceStatus`: engine, models, selected model and language, the current or last install (`job`), the engine process (poll while `job.state` is `running`) |
| `PUT /api/voice/settings` | `{ model?, language?, bin? }` → `VoiceStatus` (`bin`: path to `whisper-server`, empty = find it) |
| `POST /api/voice/install` | `{ model? }` → `VoiceStatus`: installs what is missing (engine, model) in the background, then selects the model (409 while another install runs; 400 when it cannot be installed here or the disk is too full) |
| `DELETE /api/voice/install` | cancels the install |
| `DELETE /api/voice/models/:model` | deletes a downloaded model |
| `DELETE /api/voice` | deletes the downloaded models and engine (one installed with Homebrew stays) |
| `POST /api/voice/prepare` | starts the engine ahead of a transcription (sent when recording starts) |
| `POST /api/voice/transcribe` | `{ audio }` (base64 16 kHz mono 16-bit WAV, at most 120 s) → `{ text }` (409 when voice input is not set up) |

Types are in `src/shared/types.ts`.

## Adding a new agent CLI

The order of work around these steps (installing the CLI apart, what to find out about it first, recording, checking on each system, the docs) is in [Adding a coding agent](adding-a-coding-agent.md).

There are two routes. A CLI with a sign-in of its own (like Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code) gets a **native adapter**: below full access it runs in the CLI's own OS sandbox, or, with none (as Cursor and Antigravity), asks about every command (a Cursor team's own auto-run allowlist aside, which Harness Relay cannot clear). An open-source CLI that runs on the model backends gets an **open agent** adapter, almost entirely in its own file, on the generic ACP turn when it speaks ACP.

### A native adapter

1. **Types** — add the id to `Provider`, `PROVIDERS`, `PROVIDER_LABEL` and `PROVIDER_INSTALL_URL` in `src/shared/types.ts`, and what its runs can leave out of its own setup (`NATIVE_OFF_SUPPORT`, `NATIVE_ITEM_SUPPORT`; empty when it has no switch).
2. **Adapter** — create `src/server/agents/<name>.ts`:
   - a `create<Name>Parser(emit)` (or update handler) that turns the CLI's output into `AgentEvent`s (`session`, `text`, `text_end`, `thinking`, `plan`, `tool_start` with the call's `kind` when the CLI says what it does or the adapter can tell, `tool_end` with `output` / `error`, and `detail` when the CLI only says what a call was about once it is done) and remembers the final result/error;
   - an `AgentRunner` whose `run()` builds the arguments (prompt, system prompt, session new/resume, permission, model, effort), calls `runProcess()` and throws `AgentRunError` on failure (`aborted = true` when stopped);
   - approvals: when the CLI asks to do something the permission does not cover, call `o.requestApproval({ kind, detail, reason, tool })` and answer the CLI with the result (when `requestApproval` is absent, refuse).
   Keep the parser pure so it can be tested with recorded output lines.
3. **Register** it in `src/server/agents/index.ts`.
4. **Settings** — default binary name in `DEFAULT_BIN`, the variable that moves its home (`AGENT_HOME_ENV`), the variables it takes a sign-in from (`CREDENTIAL_ENV`) and, if the CLI reads an API key from the environment, `API_KEY_ENV` (`src/server/settings.ts`).
5. **Status & login** — `checkAuth()` and `LOGIN_METHODS` in `src/server/providers.ts`; the logout command in `logout()`; how much of an account's plan is used, when the CLI can say so without a prompt, in `askUsage()` (`src/server/usage.ts`, with the variables of a key its provider bills by use in `BILLED_KEY_ENV`), and what Settings says when it cannot (`usage.notReported` in both locale files).
6. **Models** — a discovery function in `src/server/models.ts` (or a static list). A CLI that can also run on the user's own endpoints of Settings → Model backends (as Claude Code, Codex, Grok and Copilot can) goes in `EndpointCli` and `isEndpointCli()`, with the API it calls and the efforts its models take there in `ENDPOINT_CLI` (`src/shared/types.ts`), and its adapter takes a member's endpoint model with `endpointRunModel()` (`src/server/backends.ts`), which fails the turn on a deleted endpoint's model.
7. **What it loads by itself** — a probe in `PROBES` (`src/server/native.ts`), its tabs in `src/web/native.ts`, and why it cannot leave out a part (`native.offNotes`) in both locale files.
8. **UI** — a color variable in `styles.css` (`--<name>`), `PROVIDER_COLOR` and `newAgent()` in `src/web/util.ts`, the key's variable and placeholder in `SettingsPage.tsx` (`KEY_ENV`, `KEY_PLACEHOLDER`), API-key help text in both locale files (`settings.apiKeyHelp`).
9. **Messages** — login method labels in `src/server/i18n.ts` (`login`), in both languages.
10. **Bug report form** — the CLI's name among the `agents` options of `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml` (`test/community.test.ts` checks them against `PROVIDER_LABEL`).

Most of these are `Record<Provider, …>`, so `npm run typecheck` names the ones you missed, among them how AgentsView reaches the CLI's sessions (`AGENTSVIEW_SESSIONS` in `src/server/agentsview.ts`: the prefix AgentsView names them with, or `recorded` when it cannot read them where the CLI keeps them, and Harness Relay sends its own record of each member's instead). It cannot name a few: the `provider` choices in the lead agent's prompts (`src/server/planner.ts`), the model family of a CLI's own models, for when it lists none of them (`CLI_FAMILY` in `src/server/roleGuides.ts`), and how its model ids name their family (`modelFamily()`, with a guide in `ROLE_GUIDES` for a family the lead has none for yet), the CLI's own folder in `agentFolders()` (`src/server/protect.ts`), whether its instruction files are the ones it is documented to read when it cannot be asked (`memoryFromFiles` in `failedProbe()`, `src/server/native.ts`), whether an account besides the default one can keep a sign-in of its own (`keyOnlyAccount()` in `src/shared/types.ts`), the variables of its sign-in that the open agents must not inherit (`INHERITED_BACKEND_VARS` in `src/server/backends.ts`), the project folders it loads hooks or MCP servers from, which the relay sandbox keeps read-only in edit mode (`PROJECT_AGENT_CONFIG` in `src/server/relaySandbox.ts`) and edit-mode writes ask about in the Antigravity and Muse Code adapters (`AGENT_CONFIG` in `src/server/agents/antigravity.ts`), and, for a run that gets a folder of its own in `data/tmp`, how that folder's name begins, so that a start removes those of turns the server was stopped during (`RUN_TMP_PREFIXES` in `src/server/protect.ts`).

The newest worked examples are Copilot and Muse Code. Copilot (`copilot.ts`): ACP in the CLI's own sandbox, whose policy each run checks before the prompt (the CLI's own answer to `/sandbox policy`), a home of its own for each run, the user's own endpoints too, and its tests replaying messages recorded from the real CLI (`test/fixtures/copilot/`, scrubbed) through a stand-in (`test/fake-copilot.ts`). Muse Code (`muse.ts`): ACP in the CLI's own sandbox, a config folder of its own written for each run (its permission profile can be chosen only in its settings file), the session's mode set on every turn, the briefing as its developer prompt, and its tests made the same way (`test/fixtures/muse/`, `test/fake-muse.ts`). Antigravity (`antigravity.ts`) is the worked example of one without a sandbox: ACP, settings of its own written for each run, the briefing in its system prompt, and its tests made the same way (`test/fixtures/antigravity/`, `test/fake-antigravity.ts`).

### An open agent

An **open agent** runs on the model backends, with no sign-in, in a home of Harness Relay's own and, below full access, in the relay sandbox. Read [Open agents](open-agents.md) first: what the five share, and one section per agent, each a worked example.

1. **Types** — its id in `OpenProvider` / `OPEN_PROVIDERS` and `PROVIDER_LABEL` in `src/shared/types.ts`, and an entry in each `Record<Provider, …>` (the typechecker lists them: `DEFAULT_BIN`, `PROVIDER_COLOR`, the locales' notes, `NATIVE_OFF_SUPPORT` / `NATIVE_ITEM_SUPPORT`, the native panel's tabs…).
2. **Adapter** — `src/server/agents/<name>.ts` exports an `OpenAgentDefinition`, registered in `openAgents` (`index.ts`). Its `process` (`OpenAgentProcess`) declares its home: `home(root, state)` gives the variables that point the CLI there, its `configPaths` in the shared home, and its `statePaths`, everything a run writes, inside `state`, the conversation member's own folder (`<home>/private/<key>`; a sandboxed run does not start otherwise), with caches that load code in the run's temp folder or off; `prepare` (writes the CLI's config before each run: provider, model, permission rules, briefing; keys only in variables, never in a file), `proxyEnv` and `emptyEnv`, and `sandbox` for any opening it needs (each one documented in its section of `docs/open-agents.md` and in `docs/sandboxing.md`). Leave `implemented: false` until it is checked against the real CLI: until then its runs fail at once.
   - **ACP**: fill in an `AcpAgentSpec` and use `acpRunner()`; every difference from the generic turn is one of its hooks (mode, model, effort, briefing, `describeCall`, `rejection` / `afterRefusal`, `filterUpdate`…). Configure the CLI to ask, so that `session/request_permission` reaches Harness Relay's decision.
   - **Any other protocol**: use `startOpenProcess()` (it reads the backend, makes the home and the run's temp folder, runs `prepare` and wraps the command in the relay sandbox) and speak the protocol yourself, sending every action through `acpPermissionDecision()` → `requestApproval` as the ACP agents do (see `pi.ts`).
3. **Models** — the definition's `efforts`, and `models` when the CLI has a catalog (only the configured backends' models are kept, as `<backend>/<model>`; each endpoint's are added).
4. **What it loads by itself** — the definition's `native` hook, and what it can leave out in `NATIVE_OFF_SUPPORT` / `NATIVE_ITEM_SUPPORT`.
5. **UI** — a colour (`--<name>` in `styles.css`, `PROVIDER_COLOR`) and its note on its Settings card (`settings.open.notes` in both locales).
6. **Tests and docs** — `test/<name>.test.ts` replays messages recorded from the real CLI (`test/fixtures/<name>/`, scrubbed) through a stand-in, and checks the configured permissions; add its section to `docs/open-agents.md` (both languages), with the version it was checked with.
7. **Bug report form** — the CLI's name among the `agents` options of `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml` (`test/community.test.ts` checks them against `PROVIDER_LABEL`).

## Database changes

The schema lives in the `MIGRATIONS` array in `src/server/db.ts`; `PRAGMA user_version` records how many have been applied. To change the schema:

1. **Append** a new SQL string to `MIGRATIONS` (never edit a released one).
2. Update the row types and queries in `store.ts` / `settings.ts`.
3. Add or update tests in `test/store.test.ts`.

Each migration runs in its own transaction at startup. node:sqlite cannot bind `undefined`; use `nul()` for optional values, and `transaction(db, fn)` for multi-statement writes (it doesn't nest).

To inspect data:

```bash
sqlite3 data/relay.db '.tables'
sqlite3 data/relay.db 'select seq, author_kind, agent_name, status from messages order by conversation_id, seq'
```

## Changing prompts

- `buildBriefing()` — the system-level briefing (role, team, mode rules, workspace). Rebuilt every turn. `scheduleRules()` explains messages a schedule sent (`via="schedule"`), and with `unattended` (the run refuses approvals) says so.
- `buildTurnPrompt()` — the per-turn message with `<chat>` and the cue line.
- `forwardText()` — how a reply is shown to other agents (text + `<actions>` log).
- `parseMentions()` — mention parsing; text may follow a name directly, and code spans and fenced blocks of any kind (also unclosed ones) are ignored.
- `buildPlannerPrompt()` / `PLANNER_BRIEFING` (`planner.ts`) — what the lead agent is asked in auto-team mode, and the JSON it must return. For a schedule's message (`schedule`) it says the team gets it on a schedule, unattended, and asks for members named after the names it @mentions.
- `buildRevisePrompt()` / `REVISER_BRIEFING` (`planner.ts`) — what the lead agent is asked when the user wants the team in the team drawer changed: the request, the team, the conversation so far, a waiting design (for a question or the schedule), the schedule's message; it must return the whole team, naming a renamed or moved member as `was` (never a new member) and giving a role only for a new member or one it rewrites. It also says when the schedule is off or was deleted, and which saved folder the lead works in when the drawer names another one.
- `ROLE_GUIDES` / `roleGuidesForLead()` (`roleGuides.ts`) — how to write a role for each model family, which both prompts above give the lead agent where it writes roles (after `ROLE_RULE`, the rule for every role: its first line says who the member is, which is all teammates see of it, `roleSummary()`): only the guides of the families the signed-in CLIs can run (`familiesForLead()`: every model each one lists, as its new members' account runs it, and its default; for a CLI that lists none of its own models, its own family, `unlistedFamily()`), for the conversation's permission (read-only: no asking for edits), and none when no family is known. With guides, the lead's list of each CLI's models marks each model with its guide (`guide: Claude`, `guideMark()`, from `familyOf()`), and a model of no family (Cursor's Auto) with none. `modelFamily()` reads a model's family from its name (without the API or endpoint it is on); a model on the user's own endpoint is open weights, unless it is Claude, GPT, Gemini or Grok through a gateway, and an open agent's model through a model API whose name says nothing is that API's (OpenAI's GPT, Anthropic's Claude, xAI's Grok). Each guide holds only what changes how a role is written for that family, not what holds for every model: keep them short. They were surveyed on **2026-11-14** from each family's own guides: look there first when a family changes, and write the new date here. The sources, and how each family's stood then:
  - **Claude**: [prompting best practices](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/claude-prompting-best-practices) with a page for each current model next to it (Fable 5.2, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5), and [Claude Code best practices](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/best-practices). All current.
  - **GPT and Codex**: [prompt guidance](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-guidance), with a page for each model up to the GPT-6 family that Codex offers now, the [Codex prompting guide](https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/gpt-5/codex_prompting_guide) and the [GPT-5 prompting guide](https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/gpt-5/gpt-5_prompting_guide). The older GPT-5-Codex prompting guide's address answers 404, so its "less is more" advice was read from search results; no -codex model came after gpt-5.3-codex.
  - **Gemini**: [prompting strategies](https://aistudio.google.com/docs/prompting-strategies) (Gemini 3.x) and Antigravity's [subagents](https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents). Gemini 4 has no guide that could be read (its developer guide shows only with JavaScript), so Gemini 3.x's advice stands for it; Google took the [Vertex AI Gemini 3 prompting guide](http://web.archive.org/web/20260515024402/https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/start/gemini-3-prompting-guide) down (archived copy).
  - **Grok**: xAI's [best practices](https://docs.x.ai/developers/model-capabilities/text/best-practices) (Grok 4.5 to 4.7) and [project rules](https://docs.x.ai/build/features/project-rules). There is no prompting guide for Grok 4.x; the [grok-code-fast-1 prompt guide](http://web.archive.org/web/20260328045810/https://docs.x.ai/developers/advanced-api-usage/grok-code-prompt-engineering) went when that model was retired on 2026-05-15 (archived copy of March 2026).
  - **Muse**: no prompting guide for the hosted Muse models Muse Code runs (Meta's guides cover only its open-weight Muse models); from Meta's [Build with Muse Spark](https://dev.meta.ai/resources/blog/build-with-muse-spark/) and [multi-agent orchestration](https://dev.meta.ai/docs/cookbook/multi-agent-orchestration).
  - **Composer**: no prompting guide of its own; from Cursor's [agent best practices](https://cursor.com/blog/agent-best-practices) and [subagents](https://cursor.com/docs/subagents).
  - **Open-weight models**: no one guide; from the models' own cards, Goose's [benchmark of local models](https://goose-docs.ai/blog/2025/03/31/goose-benchmark) and OpenHands' [local LLMs](https://docs.openhands.dev/openhands/usage/llms/local-llms).

  Copilot runs models of these families, so its members follow their guides.
- `buildGroupPrompt()` / `GROUPER_BRIEFING` (`grouping.ts`) — how the lead agent is asked to sort a new conversation into the user's groups.

Prompts are in English on purpose (agents reply in the user's language). User-facing text is translated — see [Translations](#translations).

## Translations

The UI and the server's user-facing text come in Traditional Chinese (`zh-TW`) and English (`en`).

- **UI strings** — add the key to `src/web/locales/zh-TW.ts` first: its shape is the `Messages` type. `en.ts` is typed `Messages`, so TypeScript reports a missing or mistyped English entry; text with values is a function (`(n: number) => …`). In components use `const t = useT()` (or `useI18n()` for the language and `setLang`). Code outside React gets the messages passed in (`relativeTime(ts, t)`, `conversationMarkdown(conv, t)`).
- **Server strings** — `src/server/i18n.ts` has both dictionaries; call `t()` where the text is produced. The language comes from the request (`X-Relay-Lang`) through AsyncLocalStorage and follows the runs the request starts, so do not cache translated text in long-lived objects.
- **Built-in templates** — `src/server/presets.ts` keeps names, descriptions and roles in both languages.
- **Another language** — add it to `Lang` / `LANGS` in `src/shared/types.ts`, add a locale file and a server dictionary, the template texts, and its detection rule in `browserLang()`.

`test/i18n.test.ts` checks that both UI dictionaries have the same shape (including list lengths, which types don't check).

The guided tour's steps are `tour.steps` in the locales; the element each step points at (its `data-tour` attribute) is `TOUR_TARGETS` in `src/web/components/Onboarding.tsx`, by step index. When you add, remove or reorder steps, change both locales and `TOUR_TARGETS` together. When you change the steps in a way worth seeing, raise `TOUR_VERSION` in `src/web/tour.ts` (apart from the tour, which loads only when it opens): the browser remembers which version was finished (`localStorage` key `relay:onboarded`), so people who finished an earlier one see the tour again once. `test/tour.test.ts` checks the target count and fails when the steps change, as a reminder: update the version and the fingerprint it prints (only the fingerprint for a typo fix).

## Testing

```bash
npm test               # all tests
npx vitest test/store.test.ts
```

The tests run on macOS, Linux and Windows (CI runs them on Linux and Windows). For a test to pass on each:

- Build paths with `join()` and `resolve()`, not as text with `/`: on Windows `/repo` resolves to `C:\repo`, or to the drive of the folder it is resolved from (GitHub's runners check the repository out on `D:`, with the home folder on `C:`, and the temporary folder there too except in CI's unit tests, which put it on `D:`). A function that takes a `platform` builds that platform's paths (`posix` or `win32` from `node:path`), whichever system it runs on.
- Split a PATH with `delimiter` (`;` on Windows), and write a path into a git config file with `/`: the file reads `\` as an escape.
- Skip what only macOS or Linux have with `it.skipIf(process.platform === 'win32')` (or `describe.skipIf`): permission bits, POSIX signals and process groups, `/bin/sh`, and stand-in CLIs run by their `#!` line, which Windows cannot start.
- Leave time limits to `test/timeouts.ts` and `test/setup.ts`: `vi.waitFor` waits up to 5 s unless a test says otherwise (Vitest's own 1 s is too short for a run that records its changes with git while all the test files run at once), and on Windows, which starts processes and runs git several times slower, every test's limit and every wait is six times longer; a test that sets its own limit uses `slow(ms)`. The orchestrator's tests allow 20 s each (`test/orchestrator-harness.ts`): each runs git several times, and their files run side by side.
- Wait for what a test needs (`vi.waitFor`), not for a fixed time: Windows' timers tick every 15.6 ms.
- Windows' temporary folder may be named by its short 8.3 name (`C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…` on GitHub's runners), which `realpathSync` keeps and git and the system's own real path (`realpathSync.native`, `fs.promises.realpath`) spell out. `test/setup.ts` sets `TEMP` to the name spelled out, so the tests' folders are named as the folder dialog would name them.

`test/setup.ts` also removes every `RELAY_*` variable before a test file loads its modules, so a limit or a voice folder you set for your own Harness Relay does not change what the tests see; a test that needs one sets it itself. The end-to-end servers leave them out too (`e2e/support/relay.ts`), and a spec gives its own with `serverEnv`.

Current tests:

- `test/store.test.ts` — SQLite store and settings (round-trips, each conversation's latest agent reply in the list (which and how it ended, from memory and from the database), a tool call's kind kept and one this server does not know dropped, streaming updates, crash recovery (replies left streaming aborted, the approvals they waited on expired, their running tool calls ended), cascading deletes, file permissions), sidebar groups (order, renaming, deleting a group moves its conversations to Other, automatic sorting only once and never over the user's move or into a deleted group; a family of branched conversations moved as one, also once its top is deleted, a new branch in its family's group, a branch taken along when its original is sorted; a conversation deleted in the middle of a family remembered below it (`via`, in memory and in the database, an unreadable one left out), so the family stays together; where a branch's earlier copied messages were written (`earlier`, read back, an unreadable part left out, and given `via` once the conversation that wrote them is deleted), and a conversation's title read without loading it (`titleOf`), for a deleted one the title the sidebar shows; where each conversation was branched from, and when it was made, in the list), search (every word, ignoring case, title matches first, Chinese, `%` and `_`, system notes left out, the excerpt, replies still streaming, what a branch was branched from), and API keys with a fake credential store: never written to the database, old database keys moved out and scrubbed from the files, a refusing store. Also what each CLI's runs leave out of its own setup (kept, and only what that CLI can leave out: hooks, subagents and plugins included), and the single items left out (kept as JSON, only the kinds that CLI can leave out alone, a copy, kept through other updates and cleared by an empty object). Also the CLIs' own homes: each CLI's home variable pointing into the folder in place of the inherited one, the folders created (and still pointed at, with a warning, when they cannot be), saved with AgentsView's addresses, and the inherited homes back when it is off. Also schedules: kept with the conversation (and the messages they sent), listed for the sidebar, the due ones most overdue first, and gone when removed or when the conversation is deleted. Also auto team and `autoConfirm` kept, and schema version 13 turning auto team on for conversations saved before it; whether a schedule opens a new chat each time and the chat it last opened kept, and schema version 14 leaving older schedules in their own conversation; a conversation's sidebar group read back (`groupOf`); the working directories in use, once each, the most recently used first (`listCwds`). Also shared conversations: kept the newest first with the reply each ends with, one whose conversation was deleted marked, and forgotten (schema version 15); how many files went along, their gist and a files gist left without its page kept, and older shares read back without them (schema version 16); a share put in the place of its files gist's record in one step; a branch saved with the messages it starts with in one step (nothing when one cannot be written), and where its copied messages end (schema version 17; an unreadable one left out). Also the data folder's lock: one holder at a time, freed when it closes or its process is killed, with no file beside it, and still held after garbage collection when nothing refers to its handle (as in index.ts); and the folders the open agents' homes have been in (`openAgentRoots`), kept across a change of the agent-home folder and given to their runs. Also accounts: each CLI's other accounts kept with their names, homes (in the CLIs' own folder, else in the data folder) and keys (in the keyring, moved there from the database; only their own: none of the credential variables in the server's environment, such as Claude Code's OAuth and auth tokens), an unknown account taken as the default one, a removed one forgotten with its key and the lead agent moved back to the default one; each account's own default model and effort and what its runs leave out (a new account with none, only what its CLI can leave out, copies handed out, an account that is gone taken as the default one), and the accounts there were before schema version 20 given their CLI's values; members', template members' and agent sessions' accounts kept. Also Cursor's one sign-in: `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=memory` for an account besides the default one, and for a run with a key (the account's, or the server's own `CURSOR_API_KEY` / `CURSOR_AUTH_TOKEN`), none for the default account without one; the server's own Cursor credentials for the default account only; other CLIs left alone. Also Muse Code's accounts: one besides the default one in a home of its own, with its own Meta API key only (never the server's `META_API_KEY`), marked to run on it alone; the default account keeps the server's key. Also the backend a member's session was made on (schema version 26), none for the CLI's own service. Several working directories: a conversation's others (`extra_cwds`), a turn's changes in each repository (`more_changes`), each agent's worktree of each repository and forgetting one, the list of working directories in use with all of each conversation's, migrating the worktrees made before version 21, and leaving out what cannot be read. A database the model backends migrated as 21–25 or, before the accounts, as 19–22 (unreleased builds) brought to today's numbering: today's 21 (and before the accounts also 19 and 20) run on it and its version raised by as many, what it holds kept; one of main's own at 21 left as it is.
- `test/run-database.test.ts` — reading an open agent's own SQLite database (`readRunDatabase()`, in a worker thread): the tables that are there and the queries that need them, read-only, a database it cannot read, a view or a generated column in a table it reads, a read that does not end in time given up while the server goes on, no second read of a database while one has not answered, a link or a named pipe as the database or a journal beside it never opened (not on Windows), and a process with a read that never ends still ending, on its way out and on an uncaught exception (`exitAfterRunDatabaseReads()`, `exitOnUncaughtException()`, with `process.kill` and `process.exit` spied on); and the scrub's opening (`openRunDatabase()`) refusing a rollback journal left over, a generated column, an index on an expression or with a condition and a write-ahead log over 1 GiB, running no checks, and dropping planted triggers and views.
- `test/workspace.test.ts` — git plumbing against real temporary repositories (auto init, snapshots that leave the repo untouched and leave out the CLIs' own homes inside it, also named through a link, HEAD as a snapshot's parent and none without a commit, each file's status and line counts from one diff (names starting with a colon, binary files), worktrees, apply, conflicts, CRLF / non-UTF-8 content applied byte for byte (also when the user's git converts line endings, as Git for Windows' `core.autocrlf` does), long and unusual file lists, kept snapshot refs, repositories that are too broad). Also a repository's own config (its fsmonitor, filters, textconv, one through an include) running nothing in Harness Relay's git commands, nor the drivers of a repository at a submodule (a real submodule still taken), no missing object of a partial clone fetched, and a `core.worktree` naming another folder refused, while the user's own drivers (git-lfs's) are left alone. Checking a patch without changing anything, then applying it, and a diff's paths named after a folder.
- `test/process.test.ts` — process helpers: byte-exact output, stopping subprocesses that ignore SIGTERM (also after the CLI exited, and on shutdown), a missing working directory, relative executable paths, an npm shim's Node script started on Windows with its arguments as they are; a fixed shell script (`runShellScript`) with its flags and the values it reads from its environment, one that runs too long, and a missing shell; a detached program (`spawnDetached`) that outlives the call and is left out of `killAll`, and one that does not exist. Also `runSync`: a quick probe run directly with its input, killed after its timeout even when it ignores SIGTERM, and `sleepSync`.
- `test/winLaunch.test.ts` — what is started on Windows for a CLI: found as a shell finds it (the PATH in PATHEXT's order, whatever the letter case of `Path`; of `Path` and `PATH`, the one Node passes on; a `.JS` PATHEXT names passed over); a program as it is; one not found said so, not started bare; an npm shim's program, or its Node script with the Node beside it, the PATH's or Harness Relay's (npm's current and older shims), none in the desktop app; a shim whose script or program is not there, said so; a script of the user's own with a shim-like line, a shim for another interpreter and one that runs two things, not taken for shims; Cursor's newest whole version folder by date and build time, with `CURSOR_INVOKED_AS` and the compile cache, and another script beside such folders not taken for its launcher; Muse Code's launcher (`muse.cmd`, as its installer writes it) started as it starts Muse, the `muse-bin-<version>.exe` its `.muse-version` names, and refused when that names no binary there or is not a version; a script whose program cannot be told, refused with the reason in both languages; nothing of this off Windows.
- `test/hub.test.ts` — SSE fan-out when a browser reconnects, and listeners of every conversation (`subscribeAll()`).
- `test/notify.test.ts` — what the user is alerted to (`src/shared/notify.ts`): a run that ended with a reply it did not have when it began, by how that reply ended (not one the user stopped); none for a run that ended without a reply of its own (a team designed, a run stopped before anyone answered, also with the lead agent's run ending before the team's begins); a run already running at the first look, and one shorter than the time between two looks (a reply the list did not have; not for a conversation not seen before); what the user is needed for, once, the most urgent first, from one need to another; the reply a run began after remembered while it runs; `AlertTracker` starting at its first list and again after `reset()`; which kinds a browser wants; who replied and what they said as one short line (Markdown and the ask marker taken out, long text cut), the question asked, the command an approval waits on; a run first seen going that ends without a reply of its own (none), or whose reply was being written (one); a branch seen for the first time, also one waiting for an answer, is no news.
- `test/push.test.ts` — push notifications (`src/server/push.ts`): the encryption gives RFC 8291's own example byte for byte, and a browser reads what it gets (fresh keys and salt each time); a VAPID token for the push service's origin that the public key verifies; only push services' HTTPS addresses taken, keys of the right length that are points of the curve; preferences read; a payload cut to fit one message; a `Topic` per conversation. `PushService`: keys made when first asked and kept in the database, the same pair at the next start, the private key the public one's; new keys (and no subscriptions) when the private key is missing (an earlier build kept it in the keyring) or belongs to another pair; one subscription per browser, removed with its signed-in browser and only by it, not taken over by another; a message encrypted, signed and with its headers; a subscription its push service says is gone (404, 410) or refuses (401, 403) removed and refused when given again, one that failed kept, and one whose address is not a push service's never sent to; why it has no keys said in each request's language; nothing sent to a browser whose session is past its 400 days; a browser's latest five subscriptions kept.
- `test/alerts.test.ts` — the server's watcher (`src/server/alerts.ts`): nothing sent until a browser subscribed, and the states taken anew then; each browser told what it asked for, in its language, with or without sound, a need urgently; a run between two checks, and nothing for one that left no reply; alerts held while a page of the browser is in front and dropped at that page's next poll, not dropped by the first poll of a page that came after them, kept while another page of the browser is in front, sent once the pages go to the background or stop polling, a held need the user already met dropped; the conversations read only after an event or while agents are at work; other browsers told while one push service does not answer; the text with no conversation to read from, and the test notification; what was held sent when a poll of the page says it is not in front (also when its message that it went away was lost).
- `test/push-api.test.ts` — `/api/push` through `app.request()` with sign-in: the key for signed-in browsers only; a subscription kept in the request's language, addresses that are no push service's, wrong keys and preferences refused; a test notification to its own browser only (502 and removed when its push service says it is gone), a gone subscription refused when given again (`push-gone`), one not taken over by another browser, removed only by its own browser; the page's polls (with its id) and going to the background heard; no push service: an error and 503; a subscription another browser has refused with `push-taken`; each poll's page id, in front or not.
