Also `POST /api/schedules`: a conversation with auto team, the schedule (titled after its message, or as given) and the lead agent designing for it, nothing posted; a design asked for again only once the first is stopped; nothing created (not even the folder) for a schedule that could never send, and a missing folder made only when asked. Also a folder, or the whole working directory, downloaded as a ZIP file (headers, `HEAD`, entries read back, 404s), and one too large to zip in full (more files in an ignored node_modules than the limit, lowered to 1,000 for the test): offered without what git ignores, and 413 for all of it. Also `GET /api/cwds`: the default working directory first, then those in use (a folder name with spaces too), the most recently used first, without folders that no longer exist, the default included; a `~` in the default expanded, and a folder spelled in another case listed once where the filesystem ignores case. Also sharing: the page put on the web with the conversation's title and recorded (with the reply it ends with, and none for an `upTo` that is not an agent reply); an empty page, one over 1 MB and an unknown conversation refused; `gh`'s trouble reported (with the code for a signed-out `gh`) with nothing recorded; a share removed after its gist, or kept when the gist could not be deleted, and nothing done for an id that is not a gist's. Also the files a share takes along: `…/share/files` listing each file once (the same file by two paths, from two replies and through a symlink), with its size (a binary, a blank and an empty file too) and why one cannot go along (over 50 MB, in `.git`, outside the folder by a relative or an absolute path, missing, a folder, a reply after the one the share ends with, none, or another conversation's, and every one when the share ends at a reply that is not there), never what they hold, and a list that is not one refused; a share passing the linked files on each once, their bytes as they are (a PNG's NUL bytes, an empty file), named apart (`report.md`, `report-2.md`), the page's placeholder links renumbered to them and the count and files gist recorded, and files that are not linked left behind (one too large not even read); a file the download link would not serve, or that cannot go along, refused (409 with its code) before anything goes up, as are more than 20 files, more than 100 MB (as measured, before any of it is read: unreadable files refused as too many bytes, not as unreadable), and a page that would grow over 1 MB; the files gist recorded as unfinished while the share is made, but neither listed nor removed (409) until it is done, listed after a restart, and replaced by the share once the page is up, forgotten when it was deleted again, and kept listed when it was left on GitHub or the share stopped unforeseen; a share with files that fails without leaving anything recorded as nothing; a files gist left without its page listed as unfinished and removed alone; and removing a share deleting both gists, the files first, kept listed when one could not be deleted and forgotten once both are gone; branching from a reply (the new conversation; the user's messages, a reply still being written and shutdown refused). Also accounts: added, renamed (the default one too), listed, limited to ten per CLI, names checked; `GET /api/limits` and the limits it gives kept to (accounts, and the members of a team and a template); their keys (a Codex key signs that account in); signing each in and out on its own, and accounts a CLI does not have refused; a browser sign-in refused (400) for a Cursor account besides the default one; each account's defaults and what its runs leave out set apart and validated on the account route (the CLI's route refuses them), without asking the CLIs again; what a CLI loads by itself asked, and its files opened, for the account `?account=` names; a Codex key and an account's defaults in one request (nothing saved when the sign-in fails); each account's plan usage (`GET /api/providers/:provider/usage`), asked again with `refresh=1`, an account or a CLI it does not have refused; `GET /api/models` with one entry per account (Cursor's too); removing one (forgotten before it is signed out, so a turn that starts meanwhile runs as the default account; signed out with its own environment, its folder deleted, the lead agent back on the default one; never the default one, nor while a conversation runs); members' and the lead agent's accounts kept, anything else dropped; Grok's folder trust recorded for every Grok account, all or none when one cannot be written, undone for all, and copied to a Grok account added later. Also `GET /api/about`: the version, whether the desktop app started the server, the system and Node. Also a problem report's screenshots: uploaded named by their place, deleted again only when uploaded here (and once), what is not an image refused before anything goes up, and `gh`'s trouble passed on with its code. Also `/api/update`: the version alone until the desktop app says more, a check passed on to it and answered once it has checked, and an update passed on only when there is one to update to (409 saying so, or that one is under way). Several working directories: each once and at most eight, those missing named in `paths` and created when asked (also on `PATCH`, which keeps a conversation's others without a `cwd` and takes a `cwd` alone as the only one), edit mode refused in Harness Relay's folder after the first too, and several default ones, which new conversations and the list get; `/api/gh/login` starts, shows and stops gh's sign-in, behind the same Host and content-type checks.
- `test/prompt.test.ts` — `@mention` parsing (text right after a name, code fences), `[PASS]`, forwarding of failed / stopped / passed replies, the log of actions forwarded with a reply (Codex's file changes included), the parallel briefing, how the briefing tells agents to show media and to link files for the user to download. Also the memory and skills sections of the briefing, which says the CLI's own skills work as usual only while the user has not left them out, and lists no skills to a Claude Code agent left with no tool that reads a file. Also messages a schedule sent (`via="schedule"`), and the briefing of a conversation with a schedule (ask only when there is no reasonable answer; approvals refused unless the schedule asks), and of a chat a schedule opened, which has no schedule of its own (approvals refused only in a run nobody may be watching); an agent that asked is told a scheduled message among the new ones is not the reply; a branch's agents told that files may have changed since the copied messages. Also that every member is told the chat holds Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Muse Code and the open-source agents. The briefing names every working directory (shared, or each in its worktree with its snapshot, and each teammate's snapshots by folder), and a turn's changed files come in a block for each repository it changed, named when there are several.
- `test/community.test.ts` — the bug report form names the CLIs, modes and permissions as the app does (`PROVIDER_LABEL`, both locales' labels), and the code of conduct and the security policy give the address the app sends problem reports to (`REPORT_EMAIL`), in both languages.
- `test/commands.test.ts` — reading shell command lines: publishing commands in their many forms, "always allow" keys that do not let embedded commands through, and a command given as an argument list read as the line it runs (`commandLine`).
- `test/chat.test.ts` — ChatView logic: when Enter sends (not on a phone or tablet), when to reconnect the event stream, which worktree result can be applied, the team drawer's draft and the team it edits (a design waiting for confirmation, or the current team), what the drawer sends the lead agent (the members, how they take turns, its permission and folder) and changes (the members and how they take turns, nothing else), what changed (each kind; an empty model or space around a role is no change, and the drawer keeps its own members then), when the lead's answer is ignored (the team loaded again, or not savable), the button held a moment after it switches between Ask and Stop (a double-click or a late click on Stop does nothing), and the focus moved to the request box after an answer only when it would be lost, asking before creating a missing working directory; how a message's tool calls and thinking are grouped into one activity row (what splits a row, its counts, its key while it streams, a thought's first and latest line, which details are paths that lose their start rather than their file name when they do not fit, including every long path the server cut); the conversation a branch comes from (its title now, or its title then once deleted); when the messages follow new output (once the reader reaches the bottom, give or take a fraction of a pixel, or scrolls down to near it while more comes in; not once the reader scrolls up, however near the bottom, nor while the reader scrolls down short of that, nor when the browser moves the list down to keep the reader's place as content above grows, a pixel of rounding included; still while more comes in below and the list has not moved, and when shorter messages leave it at the new bottom). With several working directories, a result can be applied while every repository it changed still has the worktree it was made in, and is stale once any of the agent's worktrees needs a reset.
- `test/markdown.test.ts` — mention chips follow the server's rule and skip code and links (also Chinese right after a bare URL); a bare URL written right against Chinese text ends where the Chinese starts; images are shown as links; local images and videos are shown through the app; links to local files (also `file:` URLs, reference links and files written as images) become downloads through the app (a line number or anchor dropped, never another computer's share), while links to sites, anchors and folders stay as they are; reference images of local media are shown; a link is checked with HEAD first (found, missing, or unknown when the server cannot say), and a missing file keeps the link and its text with the reason after it; the check waits a moment and is dropped for a URL that changed; a click on a missing file checks again and clicks once more when it is there, without a loop; the user's own messages keep their line breaks; table cells stay on one line or wrap at a readable width, so wide tables scroll sideways.
- `test/orchestrator-harness.ts` — not a test file: what the orchestrator's tests below share. A fake runner swapped into `runners` (a fake claude CLI for the members, the lead agent and the group sorter, steered by `fake`), a new database, store, orchestrator and project folder before each test, and a time limit of 20 s a test (`slow()`, so longer on Windows), as each one runs git several times. The tests are split by topic into the files below, which Vitest runs side by side (the tests of one file run one after another).
- `test/orchestrator.test.ts` — full runs with the fake runner: requests that arrive while a run is set up or a result is applied, agents asking the user (the team stops; the one that asked answers first after the reply, in roundtable, parallel and relay; Continue skips it; a reply to someone else, or an edit, starts afresh; messages written meanwhile wait for the answer), messages sent while the team replies (delivered before the next turn of an agent they are for, in the order sent; a follow-up run for what no turn took; kept after Stop; sent first by an interrupt, the next message or Continue; withdrawn; kept across a restart), deleting a conversation mid-run, keeping a session when a fresh start fails too, and telling teammates what a failed turn changed. How many conversations have agents at work (`busy()`, what an update of the desktop app waits for): each replying one from the moment its run is set up, not one waiting for the answer to its question; the lead agent changing a team or sorting a conversation, a change being made to it, and a branch being made. Also passing what the user turned off of a CLI's own setup (all of a kind and single items) to every member's run, and to the lead agent's and the group sorter's. Also telling AgentsView after every turn and at the end of the run (with the agents' sessions known by then), linking the lead agent's and the group sorter's sessions (and sending again when the sorter ends after the run), telling it nothing about a conversation deleted while it replies, and a run kept whole when telling fails. Also accounts: each member runs in its account's home, a member moved to another account starts a fresh session, an account removed since counts as the default one (its session goes on); the lead agent runs as its account, and the members it designs get a signed-in account; each member runs with its account's default model and effort (its own first) and leaves out what its account leaves out, and the lead agent, the group sorter and a team revision with the lead's account's. Also approvals with a fake agent (asking and waiting, "always allow", a decline note, expiry on stop, refusing automatically), retrying a reply, editing a user message (`resend`: later messages are dropped and every agent starts a fresh session), attached files (saved with safe names, images and paths delivered to each agent, kept when a message is edited, deleted with dropped messages, files-only messages, bad uploads), and a tool row taking the detail a CLI only gives when the call is done. Also a turn in an open agent's home other than its latest run's (the agent-home folder changed, or back in an earlier one) starting fresh with that folder emptied, and a deleted conversation's state removed from every home it has been in. Also a member whose model moves between the CLI's own service and an endpoint, or to another endpoint, starting a fresh session with the whole history, while another model of the same backend resumes it (the backend is recorded with the session). Also every member's reply added in the team's order in parallel mode, whichever CLI each runs on. Also each member's state key (the same at every turn, another one in another conversation, the lead agent's and the group sorter's own), a permission that went up starting a fresh session with the whole history and an open agent's state folder emptied by the orchestrator (recorded per CLI in the database: back on a CLI whose last run was read-only empties its folder; a folder that cannot be emptied stops the turn and is emptied at the next one; a session never recorded counts as read-only; Claude Code only starts afresh) while one that went down resumes, a member's state removed with its conversation, a request after its turn ended refused without a card or a lock, and an open agent without a model running on its picker's default (Settings' first, a member's own before that).
- `test/acp-turn.test.ts` — the generic ACP turn against a fake ACP agent (`test/fake-acp.ts`): updates → `AgentEvent`s (text, thinking, plan, tool calls with their kind (ACP's, or the spec's `toolKind`) and their output, usage and cost; the cached tokens and the reasoning counted in the input and output as the answer's total says the CLI counted them, `acpTokens()`), the model by `session/set_model` or a config option and the effort, only when they differ, or not at all when the config sets them, the session mode for the permission, a refused value; the backend's keys and the home reaching the process, extra session fields; `session/resume` or `session/load` as advertised (the replayed history not shown), and a failed or impossible resume failing before anything is said; a permission request asked, allowed, declined with a note handed to the model, or refused automatically, edit asking only about pushing, the briefing explaining refusals when the agent takes no reason, `describeCall` seeing earlier and recent calls (a request that names no call), `afterRefusal` after the answer and `afterSession` before the prompt; requests for files and terminals never done (refused, or answered as the spec says); `filterUpdate`, `emptyEnv`, `exitGraceMs`; rows left open closed when the turn ends; stopping (`session/cancel`), a stop at any step before the prompt (the sandbox check, `prepare`, `afterInitialize`, `session/new`, `afterSession`, mode, model) ending the turn without sending it, a request the user was asked about answered `cancelled` when the turn is stopped meanwhile, a crash, the agent ending the turn itself, the timeout; an adapter not done or a model that cannot run refused before anything starts. Also the open agent process: the relay sandbox's spec (writes, read-only config, protected paths, the backend's host), each run's own temp folder (removed after), proxy variables only in the sandbox, failing closed when the sandbox cannot start and on native Windows. And the pieces: the permission decision by kind and command (an argument list read as its command line), the approval card (read-only stays a boundary; a request that names nothing gets no "always allow"), the options chosen (never an "always" one, by id when the spec says), MCP servers in ACP's form, usage, the prompt (briefing, images, a leading `/`), a to-do tool's plan. Also a turn its model refused failing with the rows it left open closed as not finished. A run with other working directories hands them to the sandbox (`extraCwds`).
- `test/cline.test.ts` — the Cline adapter: its home and the run's own `CLINE_DIR` (briefing rules file, global, MCP and provider settings, the feature-flag cache) for each backend kind × permission, its environment (local session backend, telemetry and flags off, only `CLINE_API_KEY`, its own variables emptied) and arguments (`--auto-approve`, `--thinking`), the relay sandbox's spec and opt-in; whole turns replayed by a stand-in (`test/fake-cline.ts`) from messages recorded with the real `cline --acp` (`test/fixtures/cline/`): text, reads, `apply_patch` and editor edits, commands, approvals allowed and declined, refused automatically, failed calls reported `completed`, web search and fetch, thinking, MCP from its settings file, full access, stopping (also while a permission request waits: answered `cancelled`), `session/load` (history dropped, usage from its session record), a resume that fails, a bad key, model or endpoint, a crash; its tool calls, outputs and errors; its catalog from `session/new` and what it loads by itself from `cline config <kind> --json`.
- `test/backends.test.ts` — model backends: `<backend>/<model>` ids; the store without a credential store, with one (`backend-<id>-api-key`, `endpoint-<id>-api-key`, never in the database), moving keys from the database into it and scrubbing them, a store that refuses or cannot be read, the endpoint limit; a run's backend (model, only its own backend's key, endpoint, hosts) and what it says when a model cannot run; an open agent's environment (the configured keys instead of inherited ones, its own home in the data folder or the agent-home folder); masks (the last four characters of a long key only) and Harness Relay's own address refused as an endpoint however it is spelled; the HTTP API (masked keys, setting and removing them, endpoints and what is refused about them); an open agent's status (installed, its adapter done, a backend set up; no sign-in) and models (its catalog's for configured backends, the endpoints' typed models); the endpoint form's logic. Also the model menus and the agents' status refreshed after a change that failed part way (an endpoint saved while the credential store refused its key). Claude Code, Codex and Grok on the endpoints: which models run on one (`endpointModel()`; their own ids, with a `/` too, stay theirs), the endpoint's key in its variable (one inherited taken out), the efforts sent there, the endpoints' models after each CLI's own in its menu, with the effort its "default" runs, and Claude Code's menu asked again after the endpoints change; their runs get the endpoints, never the API backends' keys. Also the endpoints deleted in Settings: remembered after a restart until one of the same id is added again, given to Claude Code's, Codex's and Grok's runs (not Cursor's), a model of one failing the run (`endpointRunModel()`) while any other model, one with a slash too, goes to the CLI as it is; Cursor left out (no endpoint models in its menu, no backends for its runs, and its menu kept when the endpoints change, while Claude Code's, Codex's and Grok's, every account's, are dropped); and an open agent's environment without `CURSOR_API_KEY`, which Cursor's keeps. Also the deleted endpoints' ids listed in the view and forgotten when asked (the route too: 404 for one not remembered, 400 for a bad id), so that a CLI's own model with the same start goes to it again, and a deleted endpoint still remembered when adding it again could not be saved. After such a failed save the endpoint is not kept in memory either, and adding it again then clears its id in memory and on disk. An add or an edit whose save fails keeps nothing of it, in memory or in the credential store: an added endpoint's key does not stay behind, an edit's endpoint and key are as they were. After a failed edit replaced a key the credential store could not give at startup, the endpoint shows no key, not an unreadable one (when the store will not take the new key out either, the warning says it still holds that key, not that the key is gone); an edit that left the key alone keeps it marked unreadable.
- `test/openhands.test.ts` — the OpenHands adapter: the run's own home and settings (a whole agent per backend kind and permission, no key in the file, subagents only with full access, the tool library in `mcp.json`, the terminal shell's `.bashrc`, tmux or a plain pseudo-terminal, the variables a project `.env` could set), cleaning up its tmux server, its approvals (the announced calls, a request about several asked about unless the permission covers all of them, a request with no call announced never allowed by itself, actions an earlier turn left rejected before a resume, subagents refused below full access, its own option ids), its updates (rows, thoughts and replies cleaned, results from observations, usage less the session's earlier count), its errors, turns replayed from the real CLI (`test/fixtures/openhands/`), its LiteLLM model catalog and what it loads by itself. Also a message that only looks like its notice of a failure held back and shown once anything comes after it or the prompt answers, in its place. Also its terminal's control sequences (bracketed paste, colours) taken out of a command's output. On Windows its home is its `USERPROFILE` too.
- `test/relaySandbox.test.ts` — Harness Relay's own sandbox, offline: what each permission may write, the protected and config paths, the opt-ins (a pty, the run's Unix sockets and trustd on macOS only, quietly denied hosts, connect-only ports), each run's own config and host temp folder readable inside the private roots, host names never looked up by the server (asked about like any other, a literal local address refused without asking), the failure message said once (packages named on Linux only), backend hosts (loopback and local destinations, a port required on this computer, never Harness Relay's), refusals before a run (patterns, edit mode in Harness Relay or the home folder, writable folders that hold what decides how agents run), the environment (only loopback kept out of the proxy, or only the agent's own ports when a backend is on loopback, which the proxy lets through on macOS only), asking about a host through the right run's approvals and keeping two runs apart, run folders and their marker file, the startup sweep of what a killed server left running, the agent started through the fixed exec script with its arguments and variables in the environment (run for real), the host process and its control channel, the Node that runs the host (the server's, or in the desktop app one on `PATH`), and failing closed (native Windows, missing programs, a desktop app without a new enough Node.js on `PATH`, a processor without the seccomp helper). It never starts a real sandbox: `scripts/sandbox-check.ts` does. Also the other spellings of a local address (`127.1`, `2130706433`, `0x7f.0.0.1`, `0177.0.0.1`, a trailing dot, `0`, the metadata address as a number) read as the address the proxy dials, refused without asking, and a name asked about once whatever its case; the host's temp folder in the data folder's `srt/`, a data folder too long for its sockets refused (no folder in the system's temp folder); the startup sweep keeping everything, with no permission given back, while a sweep found a survivor or could not tell. Also the loopback ports a run may reach relayed onto the sandbox's own loopback through the proxy on Linux (`socat` started by the fixed exec script with the ports, its path and the run's proxy credentials, percent-decoded, from the environment), and taken from the run's hosts. Also that a project's `.cursor` folder stays read-only in edit mode, with the other agents' configuration (`PROJECT_AGENT_CONFIG`): Cursor, which has no sandbox, would read rules, subagents, skills or an MCP config planted there; Antigravity's `.agents` and its other spellings (`.agent`, `_agents`, `_agent`) too, whose hooks and MCP servers it runs outside any sandbox; Copilot's in `.github` (`.github/hooks`, which it runs without asking, `.github/copilot`, `.github/mcp.json`, `.github/lsp.json`, `.github/extensions`, `.github/agents`), only where a `.github` folder exists, so that the folder can still be made; and Muse Code's `.muse`, whose hooks it runs outside its sandbox in a workspace it trusts. Also the conversation's other working directories (`extraCwds`): written as the first in edit mode only, each one's project agent config read-only, and each refused in edit mode where the first would be. A folder the run may write that overlaps voice input's folder is refused naming that folder.
- `test/sandboxProcs.test.ts` — finding a relay-sandbox run's processes on macOS by their sandbox: this user's processes from `ps`, the kernel asked through the system Python about the run's own folder against the folder around it, nothing told off macOS or without the command line tools (the marker sweep is then all there is at a run's teardown; the server's start keeps the folders killed runs left), a folder that is gone, kills in rounds until none is left (a process that forks while it is killed), and the Apple-silicon calling convention for the variadic `sandbox_check`.
- `test/relaySandbox-queued-ask.test.ts` — a network ask the relay sandbox queued behind another is refused, never put to the user, once the turn has ended (with the real orchestrator: no card left pending, no directory lock left taken).
- `test/relaySandbox-leftovers.test.ts` — on macOS, disposing of a run sweeps what still holds its marker before removing it (the host may have been killed before its own sweep), and the startup sweep finds holders by the marker; a run that fails in `sandboxCommand` leaves no marker behind.
- `test/opencode.test.ts` — the OpenCode adapter: turns replayed from messages recorded with the real CLI (`test/fixtures/opencode/`, by a fake `opencode` in `test/fake-opencode.ts`: text, thinking, tools and their rows, the to-do list as the plan, approvals per permission, `fs/write_text_file` answered and never done, cancel, resume and a failed resume, a bad key and bad models, MCP servers); its home (variables, the read-only config folder kept as written, console accounts, sessions' permission rules and saved permissions removed from its database), the run's config per backend and permission (escaped `{env:}` / `{file:}`), the project's instruction files, the order of mode, model and effort, refusal reasons handed over through its server, the turn's tokens and cost from its database (the `step-finish` parts of the turn's model calls, a subagent's sessions' too, those from before the turn or a stopped one left out, the cache and reasoning added in; the parts there before a turn read without their data, as one JSON array of ids (text ids only; a session with more than 200,000 given up on), the turn's added up in SQL (numbers past 2^63, 1e400, text and negative values); a view, a link or a named pipe in place of the database or its log refused, by the scrub too; built with `test/opencode-db.ts`), its errors, its catalog and what it loads by itself. Also the session permission rules and saved permissions taken out of its database before each run, with the triggers and views planted to put them back dropped (a generated column that keeps them throws), and the member's state started over, empty, when they cannot be taken out; a line continuation and a `function NAME` header read as OpenCode reads them; and `relay-shell` looking for the evaluating forms outside single quotes only where they can be told apart (`awk -F= '/^[A-Z_]+=/ {print $2}'` runs; a declaration's or `let`'s quoted argument, a quote in double quotes or a comment, and one left open are still screened; `let`, and `$(( … ))` beside a bracketed `$`, are evaluating forms, while `$(( … ))` beside a quoted `$` alone runs). Also refused below full access on Linux before anything starts (its server could not be watched there). On Windows it keeps its own shell (no `relay-shell` in its config).
- `test/orchestrator-workspace.test.ts` — the working directory with edit permission: an edit-mode turn refused in Harness Relay's own folder (also for a conversation saved before the check), parallel worktree isolation and apply, change tracking in roundtable mode (also of a turn whose agent committed its work itself), the directory lock (also for a repository and one nested inside it), and kept snapshot refs dropped with the messages that linked to them and with the conversation.
- `test/orchestrator-folders.test.ts` — several working directories: every agent gets them all (`addDirs`), each becomes a repository and a turn records its changes in each, with one diff naming each folder; another conversation cannot write in any of them meanwhile; in parallel mode each agent gets a worktree of each, an apply applies all of them or, with a conflict in one, none; and the worktrees of a folder taken out go at the next parallel run. One folder inside another becomes one repository whichever comes first; a worktree of a folder taken off the list is neither applied nor diffed, also once that folder is gone; and the second repository's kept snapshots go with the conversation. It takes the lock on all of them for an action allowed in a read-only run, tells a schedule that another conversation writes in one, records the others' changes when one cannot be snapshotted, and marks the worktrees for a reset when a patch fails after another was applied.
- `test/orchestrator-team.test.ts` — the lead agent: auto team with a fake lead agent (a custom design that waits for confirmation, then its team answers the question with what was written meanwhile; a team set by hand as confirmation; saving it as a template; using a template; the fallback, keeping the current team when there is one; a design for each new question with the conversation so far and the current team, members who stay on keeping their sessions; the current team taking an answer, a message that @mentions a member, Continue and a retry; a team used at once without confirmation, and one that cannot start (a note, then Continue answers without a new design); the current team kept while a design waits (a retry of an earlier reply, declining the design, none to keep for a first question); confirming refused while the conversation is held; an interjection no turn took and an edit that @mentions a member going to the current team, while "stop and ask this instead" designs; Continue designing again after a stopped design; designing again after a question is edited, with what came before it as history; and planning runs that lock nothing), and the directory lock once its team is confirmed, by that team's mode. Also a design keeps the tools and account the user set for a member who stays on, and gives new members their CLI's defaults whatever tools the lead names; the lead agent's context gathered for the conversation's folder when it designs, and for the drawer's folder when it changes a team, with each CLI's tools in its prompt. Also the lead agent changing a team at the user's request (`reviseTeam`: the whole team returned, with the request and the conversation in its prompt, members who stay keeping their ids, nothing changed in the conversation, a waiting design left waiting; the drawer's permission and folder read while the saved folder is used (the same folder spelled another way is not another one); the schedule's message only while the schedule is on; a waiting design for the schedule, its message shown although the schedule is off; a member renamed with `was` keeping its id; a declined change keeping the team; empty or unusable requests and replies, and a bad name in the drawer's team, refused; the lead's CLI failing; its session linked in AgentsView; stopped by a closed request, a newer one, or deleting the conversation; a replaced request waited for, on delete too, until its lead process has ended; stopped on shutdown and waited for until its lead process has ended, with new requests refused then). Also a team saved before the team limit was lowered handed to the lead agent to change (the answer cut to the limit; a draft larger than both refused). Also sorting a new conversation into a group with a fake lead agent (by its first message only, next to the team, not without groups, never over the user's move, stopped when the conversation is deleted).

- `test/voice.test.ts` — voice input with fake downloads, processes and engine: the pinned catalogue, the background install (engine, model, check) on Linux and with Homebrew on macOS, a SHA-256 mismatch, cancelling, disk space, a custom engine path, an engine that does not start, the engine on 127.0.0.1 behind a secret path, transcription and its language, switching and deleting models, settings, and the HTTP routes. Also downloads from the folders `RELAY_VOICE_MODEL_BASE_URL` and `RELAY_VOICE_ENGINE_BASE_URL` name (a mirror serving other bytes caught by the SHA-256), and a folder `RELAY_VOICE_DIR` names: the engine serving an empty folder of Harness Relay's own (never a `public` of the user's), and removing voice input there taking only Harness Relay's files, the folder itself once empty.
- `test/voice-web.test.ts` — voice input in the browser: WAV encoding, silence detection, inserting the transcript at the caret, and what the microphone and Settings show.
- `test/tools.test.ts` — the tool library: front matter, finding skills (two levels down, a folder that is a skill itself, offered with names and whether they are added), MCP secrets in a fake credential store (masked, kept when not sent, kept under the server's id over a rename, also a rename in letter case only on a store whose names ignore case, moved out of the database, a failing store), defaults per CLI and an agent's exceptions, marked skills, and data saved before items had CLIs. Also: renaming a server or a skill keeps agents' exceptions; values that could not be moved stay in the database; values that could not be read are never saved over, and are read again once the store opens.
- `test/files.test.ts` — browsing files against a real temporary folder: paths outside it, through symlinks or into `.git` (in any letter case), listing order and the entry limit, text / image / binary previews and cutting a long file, media only for raw, any file but a folder for a download, and the folders a conversation offers. A folder reached through a link keeps the path it was asked for, and a cut listing keeps the first entries in display order. Also what a folder's ZIP file holds (`zipPlan`): named after the folder, no `.git` and no links outside, each folder once (a link that loops back is left out), empty folders kept, given up past the limits (folders count, so a tree of empty folders stops too), permission bits kept, a root named as asked, `.git.` left out, and only what git does not ignore when asked (tracked and untracked files, not deleted or ignored ones, nor tracked links that lead outside or into `.git`; what repositories inside it do not ignore; everything outside a repository). Each working directory, and each agent's worktree of each (the innermost repository's, for a folder inside another). The same files when the folder is asked for in another letter case (macOS, Windows).
- `test/media.test.ts` — which URLs in a reply are files on this computer (relative and absolute paths, `file:` URLs, `file:line`; never other schemes, other sites, anchors or folders), media types, resolving media and downloads inside the allowed folders only (not through links leading out, not in `.git` in any letter case, nothing looked up outside them, an absolute path through a link to the folder accepted), the folders a reply's files come from and in which order, why a file cannot be downloaded (not there or a folder, or outside the folders or in `.git`, also through a link), the download's file name header (RFC 5987 with an ASCII fallback), byte ranges; a copied parallel reply's worktree found in the conversation it was branched from (further up for a branch of a branch, or through an `earlier` part, never the branch's own). With several working directories, each one, inside every worktree of its repository.
- `test/zip.test.ts` — the ZIP writer, read back the way an unzip tool reads a file (`test/zipReader.ts`): folders and files with UTF-8 names, deflated with data descriptors and Unix modes, an empty file, an empty archive, a file that cannot be opened left out, a read error ending the archive, the open file closed when the download stops (right after its header too); names made safe (`\`, `..`, `:`, a leading `/`, control characters) and permission bits kept; MS-DOS times.
- `test/highlight-web.test.ts` — the file browser's highlighting (`src/web/highlight.ts`): highlight.js classes, a language by its alias, null for one it does not have, the text escaped so a file cannot put markup in the page, and only spans and their classes kept from a tree.
- `test/chunks-web.test.ts` — a part of the page that could not be loaded (`src/web/chunks.ts`): the page reloads for it once, then not again within a minute, nor where the tab cannot note that it did, nor just after a time it cannot be sure of; the part waits while the page reloads, and otherwise fails with a `PartLoadError` that keeps its cause.
- `test/files-web.test.ts` — the file browser's breadcrumb, filter, code language and line count. Also what downloading a folder as a ZIP file does: all of it, only what git does not ignore, or too large.
- `test/tools-web.test.ts` — the team editor's tool picker: items, the memory part per CLI, defaults per CLI and recording only exceptions; and what a member's Tools say about its CLI's own setup, by CLI, while the tool library is empty or has nothing for its CLI (only other CLIs' memory notes) and in the hint over its items (a CLI with a sign-in also loads its own setup, never "as usual"; an open agent does not read its own config folder in the home folder, and the text never says its skills do not load; both say that Settings lists what the CLI loads in the default working directory).
- `test/cwds-web.test.ts` — the working directory box's list: all of it when it opens or the box is empty, the paths that contain every word typed (ignoring case, in their order), folder names with spaces however much space is typed, `~` read as the home folder, and Windows paths matched whichever separator is typed, marked as the path spells them. Also how the list answers: ↑ ↓ round the list, the highlight kept on the same folder when the list is fetched again, ↑ ↓ showing every folder while nothing shows, Enter only on a highlighted folder, Escape only while the list shows (otherwise it is the dialog's), and the arrow button. Its chips: each folder once (a separator at the end makes no other), Enter adds what is typed and Backspace in the empty box takes out the last, the folder dialog opens at what is typed or the last folder, and a conversation's `cwd` and `extraCwds` from the list. None past eight.
- `test/folder-picker.test.ts` — the folder dialog: which one each system has (AppleScript, Windows PowerShell, zenity else kdialog in a graphical session, none over ssh), its command (AppleScript's title and folder as arguments after the fixed script, PowerShell's fixed script with them in its environment, zenity opened inside the folder), its answer (AppleScript's `/` at the end left out but for the root's, a name ending with a space, a BOM, Cancel, a time limit or a signal as `null`, a failure with what the program said), the folder it opens at (the nearest that exists), and that it runs with the request's signal and a time limit. Several folders at once (AppleScript's `with multiple selections allowed`, zenity's `--multiple`), one to a line, and no `activate`. A UTF-8 character set for zenity and kdialog when there is none (zenity refuses a Chinese title without one), and Windows PowerShell by its full path.
- `test/native-web.test.ts` — the panel of what a CLI loads by itself: each CLI's tabs before it is asked and their counts after (instructions and memory apart), what each CLI can leave out all at once or one by one, ticking and clearing single items, how many items its runs leave out (an MCP server with its tools once), whether what is left out changed since the answer, ticked items a fresh answer no longer lists and clearing them, and the single items as stored (`nativeItemsFor`).
- `test/native.test.ts` — what each CLI loads by itself: reading Claude Code's, Codex's and Grok's answers (recorded shapes; MCP configs and their tokens are dropped), the instruction and memory files Claude Code and Codex read (real temporary folders), a CLI that cannot be started, the cache per executable and per what is left out, the latest answer however old (for opening a reported file), problems worded per request, and which files may be read (only the reported ones). Also hooks (without their commands), subagents and plugins; marking what Harness Relay's runs leave out (not what the CLI turned off by itself); the files Claude Code and Codex skip with their variables and settings; and asking again the way Harness Relay starts each CLI, through stand-in `claude`, `codex` and `grok` executables (the same flags, overrides and variables as a run; nothing marked when that fails; a list the second answer lacks named in an error; Claude Code not started again for instruction files or memory alone; a subagent that replaces a built-in one not counted as built-in; plugins that cannot be listed). Also tools: Claude Code's built-in and MCP tools from its `/usage` turn (MCP tools by server, servers only that turn names), sent only when `/usage` is its own command (never with a skill of that name) and without a session file, and asked without the turn when it rejects `--no-session-persistence`, and again with all its skills left out (when it lists no commands); Codex's MCP tools per server from `mcpServerStatus/list` (as Codex lists them, not filtered by the config it echoes; none for a server that failed to start; a server turned off not started again; a Codex that ignored `disabled_tools` showing the tool still loaded). Items ticked that the second answer still loads (`stillLoaded`, only in lists it gave), and a second answer that runs out of time keeping what came. And single items: Claude Code's skills, built-in tools, MCP tools, a whole MCP server whose tools are taken away, subagents (a built-in one too), plugins and instruction files; Codex's skills, MCP servers and tools, hooks and plugins; each marked, through the stand-ins. Also asking again when the CLI's home moved (through a real `SettingsStore`). Also how much a probe got (`probeCompleteness`: complete, incomplete, or none because late or failed; a refused question or an unanswered part with lists answered empty is not none), and the lead's answers (`recent`): the last one at once, asked again in the background once it is old, waited for when there is none or what is left out changed, each account in its own home with what that account leaves out. Also Cursor, answered from its files without starting it: its skills from each folder it reads (the first of a name only), its MCP servers by name only (a project's marked as needing approval), its hooks by event without their commands (Claude Code's in `~/.claude/settings.json` among them), its subagents and instruction files, and nothing to leave out. Also a CLI that could not be asked (`failedProbe()`): the instruction files of Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Antigravity are the ones they are documented to read, as their probes say. Claude Code is asked with the other working directories (`--add-dir`), in both asks, and the answer is kept per folders.
- `test/agentsview.test.ts` — sending conversations to AgentsView with a fake `fetch`: the thread as a Claude Code transcript (the user's turns, replies under the agent's name and CLI, a failure noted, attachments by name, system notes and replies still streaming left out, the title last), AgentsView's session ids (Antigravity's as `antigravity-cli:<id>`, Copilot's as `copilot:<id>`, after its `session-state` folder, Muse Code's as `muse:<id>`; none for Cursor's or the open agents', a Cursor or open-agent lead agent's or sorter's neither; every CLI either named or recorded; none, and nothing thrown, for a CLI this build does not know), the members' recorded sessions (Cursor's and the open agents': what was said before each reply, as the thread shows it, without the other agents' thinking or tool output, thinking, tool calls and their results (each kind of call as Claude Code's tool for it, with its detail under that tool's key and the CLI's own name kept, a search of the web as `WebSearch`, other calls under the CLI's name), what comes after them, calls still running or failing without a reason, every kind of approval, attachments and the plan as text, empty text and thinking left out, what was heard carried over a reply that left nothing to show, `[PASS]` and failures, each reply's tokens on its last assistant message, ids of their own that stay the same, the member's model or else the CLI (also once it left the team or moved to another CLI), the `cost-state` total by model to the microdollar (also of replies with no tokens), an unknown cost said and no cost claimed where none was reported, the name (without the conversation's when it has none); only the replies on such a CLI, one session per member, uploaded after the thread in its place and linked under it, sent again only once changed, to another address, project or machine, or after a failure, linked again after its link failed without being sent again, a failed one keeping none of the others from being sent and linked while a thread that cannot be sent stops the update, one AgentsView refuses as deleted (409) neither linked nor sent again until it changes and not a failure, nothing sent, linked or kept once the conversation is deleted during the update; the thread placed with an Antigravity member's session), the upload (file name, project and machine from a known agent session or the folder's name, `allow_shorter`, the `Origin` header) and the parent links (with the lead agent's and the group sorter's sessions, Muse Code's and Copilot's among them), nothing sent without an address or a message, one update at a time per conversation with the newest state sent once after it, starting again right after an update that had nothing to do, dropping a deleted conversation's waiting update, redirects never followed, the place taken from the first agent session AgentsView answers for, and the last failure kept for its address until an update works.
- `test/limits-web.test.ts` — the server's limits in the UI (`src/web/limits.ts`): the defaults until the server answers, then its limits; listeners told only of a change (the same snapshot otherwise); a failure keeping what it had and the next ask trying again; asked again for each new listener, once at a time; a page event asking only while a component listens.
- `test/usage-web.test.ts` — an account's plan usage in Settings (`src/web/usage.ts`): a limit's name (its span, with a model it is for; its length when it is none of the usual spans; the allowance when the CLI gives neither), what is left (never below 0), its bar's colour as it fills, when it starts over (in minutes or hours within a day, else its date and time, nothing once past), what it counts when the UI names it (Copilot's premium requests, completions), its line in both languages (requests counted for a limit of requests), the credits left (or unlimited), why an account has none (a plan without limits, and Claude Code without plan limits for how it signs in, too), by CLI when its CLI does not report usage, and what went wrong (the CLI's words as they are, Harness Relay's codes in the UI's language); the reset date checked in whatever timezone the tests run in.
- `test/refresh-web.test.ts` — what the chat shows of the CLIs' status and the model catalog when answers come in out of order (`src/web/refresh.ts`): for each CLI the status checked last, whatever order the answers arrive in (the new one wins at the same time or over an undated one shown, and a dated one shown is kept over an undated new one); for the catalog, an older answer coming in last dropped, an older one kept when a newer request fails, and a plain load during a forced one asked only once that one has landed (once for all the plain loads made meanwhile, and also after a forced answer that could not be shown).
- `test/theme.test.ts` — the light or dark theme (`src/web/theme.ts`): a saved choice read (anything else is the system's), the theme shown for each choice and system mode, `<html data-theme>` set for light or dark and removed for the system's, with the browser's bar colour (`theme-color`) following; kept in the browser, and the system's when storage throws; in the page with stubbed globals: started from the saved choice, then following the system's mode, another tab's choice (by its key only) and the user's. Also the two lists of dark colours in `styles.css` the same (the system's dark mode and dark chosen), nothing else following `prefers-color-scheme`, the bar colours equal to the page's backgrounds, `index.html` setting the theme before the app's module from the same key and only to light or dark (inside `try`), and every choice named in both languages.
- `test/updates.test.ts` — the desktop app's updates in the chat: only the version when no desktop app started the server; what the app last said, with this version; a check asked of the app and answered once it has checked (or what is known after a while; none asked of an app that does not update); checks asked at once all answered when the app has answered their ids, not by a state already on its way, one not answered in time no longer waited for; the app's answers owed to the chat's checks given once no check is under way (`CheckAnswers`); the server waiting longer than the app gives GitHub; an update asked of the app only when there is a newer version and none under way (saying which). The app's own window told from another browser (a phone's); the dialog offering a version once there, not while an update is under way, opening only while no other dialog nor the tour is open, the user is not typing into a text field and nothing holds dialogs back (a voice recording), then staying while on offer, and offered again next time when closed without Later; asking often while a check, a download or an install goes on or for a while after Update (not while an update waits for the conversations); the dialogs open on the page, only the top one taking keys, and held back while a voice recording goes on (released once); and what Settings' Version card says (an update under way first, then a check, a newer version, a failed check, never checked, up to date with when). The page's watch on the server's version: it reloads once the server answers another version, asked now and then and when the window comes back, a server not answering being no change, and asks no more after.
- `test/route.test.ts` — the client's hash routes (the home page at `#/`, an unknown route too, New chat at `#/new`, and the New schedule page), including the settings sections (Version first, Notifications after Appearance), and which settings card is being read (highlighted in the links); the links making room for the button in the top-left corner as soon as their row comes up to it (`makeRoomForCorner()`), not only once stuck to the top; the row of links stays still while the highlighted one shows, and otherwise scrolls just far enough, never past either end. The model backends come before every CLI, after the appearance and the notifications.
- `test/grok-trust.test.ts` — recording Grok's trust in a folder: the entry `grok --trust` writes, replacing an untrusted one and keeping the rest, TOML escaping of Windows paths, the file in a temporary `GROK_HOME` (owner-only), putting it back as it was, reading the trust and the folder it is decided for from `grok inspect` (a project root without its trailing slash, above the folder or not, else the folder itself), and when a team design waiting for confirmation shows it (a Grok member and a folder).
- `test/util.test.ts` — switching an agent or the lead agent to another CLI and back restores the model, effort and account picked for each CLI; the model picker's "default" names what a member then runs on (an open agent without one needs a model). An agent's account: the one it names while its CLI has it, else the default one; whether it can run (signed in, unknown, or with a key); the account a new member gets (the default one when ready, else the first ready one); its name in the UI. A timeout signal aborting after its time, as `AbortSignal.timeout()` does, and not once cleared.
- `test/open-state.test.ts` — each open agent's state of its own: two conversations' runs, whose state folders neither can read or write (the relay sandbox's spec and its file rules), a key's folder kept from one turn to the next and emptied with `freshState`, a run without a key in a folder removed after it, a state path outside the member's folder refused below full access, keys as folder names (a hash for an odd one), every open agent's private folder kept from reading; each of the five adapters' layout (everything a run writes in the state folder, the CLI pointed there); only the run's own backend's key in its environment, no other model provider's inherited key, what is not a backend's still passed (`allBackendKeys` for all); a state folder emptied whatever its run did to it (a folder locked with `chmod 000`, a read-only file) without following a link out of it; a deleted conversation's state removed from every open agent's home, and what a stopped server's runs left (folders set aside included) cleared at start; a member's memory exception for an open agent kept by validation; an MCP tool's approval card (its input, read-only still a boundary). Also the private folders in the folders the homes were in before, kept from a run (from its environment) and none made there; and the start's clearing without permissions given back while something a stopped run left may still run.
- `test/e2e-fakes.test.ts` — the end-to-end tests' fake CLIs (`e2e/fake-cli/`), bundled as `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts` bundles them and run as each CLI by its file name: a module for each of the twelve, named, labelled and versioned as Harness Relay and the specs' facts name it (`e2e/support/clis/`), its `--version` answered (read as Harness Relay reads it, a sentence's full stop after it left out, as Copilot prints one); a name that is no CLI refused; each start logged by provider id, with its home; the fake lead agent's members of every CLI named as the tests name them; where the log file may not be written, as in Harness Relay's own sandbox, the line sent to the fake endpoint instead (`E2E_FAKE_LOG_URL`); the Windows launchers of `e2e/specs/windows.spec.ts` as `src/server/winLaunch.ts` starts them (Cursor's as its Node and its script, Cline's and Pi's npm shims for a Node script as the PATH's Node with its script, OpenCode's as the program it names); and the test servers' data folders short enough for the sandbox's sockets, as the server counts them (`DATA_DIR_MAX` against `hostTmpRefusal()`).
- `test/quickstart.test.ts` — the commands people copy run in any shell: npm scripts set no variables the Unix way (npm runs them with cmd.exe on Windows), the landing page, README and user guide never join commands with `&&` (Windows PowerShell 5.1 rejects it), and the README quick start starts from a fresh clone and runs the built app (`npm run build`, then `npm start`, with the sign-in link at http://127.0.0.1:8787). The landing page installs the desktop app: download buttons for each system, no command blocks and no `git clone`, `npm run dev` and `npm start` once each, and a link to the README's quick start. Also that no bare address in the zh-TW docs runs straight into the Chinese text after it, which GitHub would link along with it (write `<http://…>`).
- `test/devcontainer.test.ts` — the dev container for GitHub Codespaces (`.devcontainer/`): it forwards the port `npm start` listens on and runs its own scripts; its container options are the ones bubblewrap needs and no more (no `privileged`, capabilities, mounts or `init`; only the sshd and GitHub CLI features); `setup.sh` installs the seven CLIs that are not open source (npm's, and the makers' own installers fetched whole, given no input, and linked into `/usr/local/bin`) and no open-source agent; `start.sh` run with stand-ins for `npm` and `curl` asks the server's default address (`DEFAULT_HOST`) whether one runs, starts it with the codespace's address in `RELAY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` and its sign-in link (and the server's stderr) on that address with the code beside it, or prints `npm run login`'s link there without Vite's; it keeps the hosts already allowed, reads only the names it asks for from Codespaces' file in a shell without its profile, and changes nothing outside a codespace (with `bash`; skipped on Windows).

Good next candidates:

- **Adapter parsers** — record real CLI output into fixture files and assert the emitted `AgentEvent`s. To capture output:

  ```bash
  echo "hello" | claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages > claude.jsonl
  # Codex, Grok, Cursor, Antigravity, Copilot and Muse Code speak JSON-RPC; log what the adapter sees with a console.log in
  # JsonRpcConnection.handleLine, or copy Grok's per-session updates from
  # ~/.grok/sessions/<url-encoded cwd>/<session-id>/updates.jsonl
  ```

- **Prompt helpers** — `buildTurnPrompt`.
- **More orchestrator scheduling** — relay hand-offs and `[PASS]` early exit, with the same fake runner as the orchestrator's tests (`test/orchestrator-harness.ts`).

Running real agents in tests costs money and needs logins, so keep automated tests offline. Tests never touch the real credential store either: `SettingsStore` takes it as a constructor argument, and only `src/server/index.ts` passes `openSystemKeyring()`.

## End-to-end tests

```bash
npm run test:e2e:install                  # once: the Chromium Playwright drives (Linux: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium)
npm run test:e2e                          # every spec in e2e/specs/, in Chromium
npx playwright test e2e/specs/chat.spec.ts
npx playwright test -g approvals          # by title
npx playwright test --ui                  # pick and watch tests in Playwright's window
npx playwright show-report                # the last run's report, with the traces of failed tests
E2E_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit npm run test:e2e   # after npx playwright install firefox webkit
```

They drive the UI in a real browser ([Playwright](https://playwright.dev), [`playwright.config.ts`](../playwright.config.ts)) against the real server, `src/server/index.ts` with the built UI, so the routes, sign-in, SSE, the orchestrator, the adapters and the workspace's git are tried together, as a person uses them. The agents are fake CLIs, so the tests stay offline and cost nothing, and they run the same on macOS, Linux and Windows. [`e2e/README.md`](../e2e/README.md) lists every scenario they try, area by area, with what each one checks, which CLIs it runs with and what they do not cover yet; change it (in both languages) with every end-to-end test you add, change or remove.

- **Each test has a server of its own** (`e2e/support/relay.ts`, the `relay` fixture in [`e2e/fixtures.ts`](../e2e/fixtures.ts)): on a free port (`PORT=0`), with a temporary folder for its data (`RELAY_DATA_DIR`), a home (`HOME`, `USERPROFILE`, and the XDG folders in it, so Cursor's home is its `.config/cursor`), the CLIs' homes (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `GROK_HOME`, `COPILOT_HOME` (its `.copilot`), and Antigravity's `.gemini` and Muse Code's XDG folders in that home; the open agents' are in the data folder, `agent-homes/<cli>`) and the default working directory (a small project, `seedProject()`), and `RELAY_KEYRING=off`: nothing of your own Harness Relay, CLIs or keychain is read or changed. On macOS and Linux that folder is in `/tmp` (`E2E_TMP_DIR` names another), as Harness Relay's own sandbox needs a short data folder for its sockets and macOS's temporary folder is too deep; a test server refuses to start where its data folder would be too long. Your credentials are left out of its environment too (`ANTHROPIC_*`, `OPENAI_*`, `XAI_*`, `GROK_*`, `CODEX_*`, `GH_*`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and any `*_API_KEY` or `*_TOKEN`), and so are Cursor's, Antigravity's and Copilot's own (`CURSOR_API_KEY`, `CURSOR_AUTH_TOKEN`, `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`, `CURSOR_INVOKED_AS`, `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE`, `NO_OPEN_BROWSER`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `AGY_ADC_AUTH`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, `ANTIGRAVITY_*`), Copilot's (`COPILOT_*`: its home, model, BYOK endpoint, allow-all), your own XDG folders, and the runner's and your Harness Relay's variables (`PLAYWRIGHT_*`, `E2E_*`, `RELAY_*`, Claude Code's nesting ones), so a real CLI or `gh` found by mistake has nothing to reach a service with. Git looks for a repository no further up than the test's folder (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`), so one around it, such as a home kept in git, is never taken for the working directory's. Through the API, before the page opens, the CLIs a test does not need (`missingClis`: by default all but Claude Code, Codex and Grok, `DEFAULT_CLIS` in `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts`, as the server asks every installed CLI for its version, sign-in, models and what it loads by itself whenever a page opens, some six starts each, so that ten CLIs made a test a second slower on CI, three seconds on Windows) are given an executable that does not exist, so they are not installed there (`eachCli()`, `eachHalf()` and `withClis()` install the CLIs a test is about, `withEveryCli()` all of them); then the test's fake model endpoint (below) becomes a model backend and its first model the open agents' default, and OpenHands' terminal switch is turned on. When the test ends the server is stopped and its folder removed. When that fails (Harness Relay makes some folders in it read-only, such as OpenCode's config in its home), the owner's write permission is given back on every folder under it, without following a link, and the removal is tried again; a folder that still cannot be removed is only reported (`[e2e] could not remove …`) and does not fail the test. The fixture reads the sign-in link the server prints. The `page` fixture opens the link of a new code, so it starts signed in, with the tour done and the UI in English; `test.use()` changes that (`lang: 'zh-TW'`, `signIn: false`, `tour: true`), adds environment for the server (`serverEnv`), leaves CLIs not installed (`missingClis`), starts the server without the fake endpoint as a model backend, so that the open agents are installed but not ready (`openBackend: false`), or with OpenHands' terminal switch off (`sandboxTerminal: false`); `live: true` runs the real CLIs instead (the [live tests](#live-tests)). `relay.api()` calls the API as a signed-in browser, to set things up or check what was stored, and `relay.fakeLog()` returns what the fake CLIs were started with and given. A failed test gets the server's output and the fake CLIs' log attached, besides its trace, screenshot and video.
- **The fake CLIs** ([`e2e/fake-cli/`](../e2e/fake-cli/), CommonJS using only Node's built-ins) stand in for all twelve CLIs and speak each one's protocol as its adapter drives it: Claude Code's stream-json with its control requests and `can_use_tool`, `codex app-server`, ACP for Grok, Cursor (`cursor-agent acp`), Antigravity (`agy acp`), Copilot (`copilot --acp`, answering `/sandbox policy` without the model, as Copilot does), Muse Code (`muse acp`), OpenCode, Goose, Cline and OpenHands, and Pi's JSONL RPC (`pi --mode rpc`, with Harness Relay's real gate extension, `piGate.js`, loaded as Pi loads it); and they answer the probes the server runs (`--version`, the sign-in status, the models, `grok inspect --json`, what each CLI loads by itself), exiting with an error at once on anything else. Each CLI's fake is a module of its own (`agents/<provider>.cjs`, with its file name, label, version, home and API key check), beside what they share (`lib/`: JSON-RPC and ACP on stdio, a turn's steps, homes, files and the log), and `fake-cli.cjs` picks the module by the name the process runs under (`cursor-agent` is Cursor, `agy` Antigravity). `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts` bundles the modules into one script when it installs them (`bundleFakeCli()`, a small module loader of its own): a Node single executable's `require` loads only Node's built-in modules, and as a script the fake runs from the cache folder, where the modules are not. What the specs need to know of each CLI and its fake (its tools' names, what it asks about, how it takes a refusal, where its home is, and whether its sign-in is asked in an account's own folder: `statusInOwnFolder`, false for Copilot, which is asked in a home of the question's own with a copy of the account's sign-in state) is in `e2e/support/clis/<provider>.ts`, changed together with the fake, and `e2e/support/clis.ts` answers the specs' questions with it (`eachCli()`, the two teams of `HALVES`, `basePermission()`, `needsBelowFull()`, `defaultHome()`, `sessionFile()`). The server finds the fakes first on its PATH, by name, as it finds the real ones. On macOS and Linux each one is the bundled script with a `#!` line for the Node that runs the tests (OpenHands' in a Python virtual environment of its own, as `uv tool install` lays it out, where the server reads its models from LiteLLM's list). Windows starts only real programs (`.exe`) without a shell, so there `e2e/support/fake-cli.ts` makes the script a Node single executable application (`node --experimental-sea-config`, then `postject`), linked under the twelve names and as `gh` (below). That takes a few seconds and about 100 MB, so it is kept in `node_modules/.cache/harness-relay-e2e/` until a module or Node changes (and then for an hour after a server last started with it, for another run that may still be using it; a server refuses to start when one of its fakes is missing, rather than find the real CLIs). `E2E_FAKE_SEA=1` builds them that way on macOS and Linux too. On Windows postject warns `The signature seems corrupted!`: injecting the script breaks `node.exe`'s Authenticode signature, which the copy does not need. For `e2e/specs/windows.spec.ts`, `installFakeLaunchers()` also makes the fakes as some Windows users' CLIs come: Cursor's own launcher (`cursor-agent.cmd`, a Node and a script of Cursor's) and npm's `.cmd` shims, for a Node script (Cline, Pi) and for a program (OpenCode). [`test/e2e-fakes.test.ts`](../test/e2e-fakes.test.ts) bundles and runs the fakes with `npm test`, so a module that does not load shows before an end-to-end run.
- **What the fakes answer** depends on the prompt, as a model's would. The lead agent's prompts get a team (`e2e:team=claude,cursor,pi`, by provider id, `e2e:mode=relay`, `e2e:rounds=2`, `e2e:name=Some_name`, `e2e:template=<id>`, `e2e:badplan` for an answer that is not JSON; one Claude member by default), a group (the first group that the first message names) and a changed team (`e2e:add=<cli>` adds a member). A turn gets `Hello from <name> (<CLI>). You said: “<the user's latest message>”.`, with the images and the tool library's MCP servers it got, streamed in pieces and changed by directives in that message: `e2e:think`, `e2e:tool` (a listing of the working directory with the CLI's own tool), `e2e:todo`, `e2e:approve` (a command that needs approval; `e2e:approve=publish`, `npm publish e2e-approval`, which asks in edit mode too, for Pi and for Copilot, which runs `echo` without asking), `e2e:fetch=<url>` (reaches the URL: a host the run may not reach without asking), `e2e:write=<path>` and `e2e:writeother=<path>` (in the first other working directory the CLI was given), `e2e:ask`, `e2e:handoff=<Name>`, `e2e:slow` (about ten seconds, to be stopped; `e2e:slow=<seconds>` for a test that waits for its end), `e2e:fail` (exits with an error), `e2e:error` (fails inside the protocol), `e2e:flaky` (fails once, answers the retry; for Cursor, as a chat's first message), `e2e:markdown`, `e2e:mermaid` (a Mermaid diagram), `e2e:memory` (says the tool library's memory notes its briefing gave it) and `e2e:skills` (reads each tool-library skill its briefing names, where the CLI may read, and says its first line) (`DIRECTIVES` in `e2e/fake-cli/lib/plan.cjs`), and for Cursor alone `e2e:delete=<path>` (a delete, which Cursor always asks about). `@<cli>` after a directive keeps it to that CLI, by provider id: `e2e:write=a.txt@cursor`. A later round with nothing new from the user is `[PASS]`. Like a model that ignores its briefing, a fake always tries what a directive asks, and whether it happens depends only on what Harness Relay gave the CLI: Claude Code's `--permission-mode`, `--disallowedTools` and `--add-dir` folders, Codex's sandbox or permission profile (with its `write` entries; `writable_roots`) and approval policy, Grok's yolo mode and the `read_write` folders of its sandbox profile (without a sandbox, in yolo mode and on Windows, the other working directories its briefing names), Cursor's `--force`, approval mode and the allow and deny rules of its config folder, Antigravity's settings, mode and flags, Copilot's flags (`--sandbox`, `--allow-all`, `--add-dir`, `--excluded-tools skill`), the settings of its run's home (its sandbox's policy) and the session's `allow_all`, Muse Code's permission profile (in its config folder's settings), session mode and flags, each open agent's config, environment and the files its adapter writes, and the answers to each one's approval requests (a decline's note reaches the CLIs that take one). Cursor and Antigravity have no sandbox, so their fakes ask as the real ones do; Copilot's fake works out what its sandbox's policy allows, as the real one keeps to it (a write outside it fails without asking); the open agents' fakes write and reach URLs for real, so below full access what is not theirs is refused by Harness Relay's own sandbox itself, and they report the refusal as their CLI does. So a test that writes in a read-only chat or in another folder tells whether the adapter set the CLI up right. The fakes keep their sessions in their homes (an open agent's in its member's state folder) and refuse to resume one they never started, as the real CLIs do. They find skills of their own where the real CLIs look (Claude Code: its home's `skills/` and each working directory's `.claude/skills/`; Codex: its home's `skills/`; Grok: the project's `.claude/skills/`, only in a folder it trusts; Copilot: a project's `.github/skills/` and its home's `skills/`) and honour what Harness Relay leaves out (`skillOverrides`, `skills.config`, `GROK_CLAUDE_SKILLS_ENABLED`, Copilot's `--excluded-tools skill`). They are signed in until their own sign-out (`auth logout`, `logout`, Antigravity's and Copilot's over ACP) in their home, and `claude auth login` prints a link and waits for a code (Cursor's `login` finishes after `E2E_FAKE_CURSOR_LOGIN_MS`, or a line on its stdin; Cursor's sign-in is one per computer user, as the real one's, and a key signs a run in by itself). `relay.fakeLog()` also has each start's home and whether it got an API key, and the one the test expects (`E2E_EXPECTED_API_KEY`; never the key), each turn's model, effort and skills (and Grok's trust), and the values the tool library's MCP servers got; each line names the process that wrote it (`proc`): match a process's lines by that, not by its pid, which Windows soon gives to a new process. `E2E_FAKE_SIGNED_OUT=codex,cursor` (through `serverEnv`, by provider id) signs those CLIs out (`gh` too). `E2E_FAKE_CURSOR_TEAM_ALLOW` gives the fake Cursor a team's auto-run allowlist (command prefixes it then runs without asking).
- **The fake model endpoint** (`FakeEndpoint` in `e2e/support/relay.ts`): each test server has a small HTTP server of its own beside it, set up as the model backend `e2e` (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>/v1`, its models `e2e-model` and `e2e-model-mini`), with `e2e/e2e-model` each open agent's default model, so the open agents are ready in every test that installs them. No model answers there: it lists its models (`GET /v1/models`), and it takes the log lines of the fakes that run inside Harness Relay's own sandbox (`POST /e2e-log`, `E2E_FAKE_LOG_URL`), which may not write the log file (`E2E_FAKE_LOG`) there: the run's backend is the one host the sandbox lets a run reach, and `relay.ts` adds those lines to the same file, a moment after the reply (read them once the turn has `settled()`, or with `logged()` from `e2e/support/clis.ts`). The open agents run below full access in the real sandbox on macOS, and on Linux with bubblewrap, socat and ripgrep: `e2e/global-setup.ts` works out once whether it can work here (`E2E_RELAY_SANDBOX`: `ok`, or why not; `e2e/support/sandbox.ts`), and a test that needs it is skipped where it cannot, saying why, or fails instead with `E2E_REQUIRE_RELAY_SANDBOX=1` (as on CI). Where a CLI runs only with full access (the open agents on Windows, OpenCode on Linux), a test about permissions is skipped for it (`needsBelowFull()`), and any other runs it with full access (`basePermission()`); a test of every CLI runs as two teams, the seven with a sign-in and the five open agents, since a team has at most eight members (and one the lead agent designs by itself at most five, so `chat.spec.ts` asks for the seven in teams of four and three).
- **The fake folder dialog** ([`e2e/fake-cli/fake-dialog.cjs`](../e2e/fake-cli/fake-dialog.cjs)) is installed with them as `zenity` on macOS and Linux. Linux looks for its dialog on the PATH in a graphical session, so a test that sets `DISPLAY` (through `serverEnv`) gets the box's folder button, and the fake answers with the folders `E2E_FAKE_PICK` lists (JSON, each resolved from the folder it opens at), or is cancelled when it lists none. Its arguments and character set go to `relay.fakeLog()` (`cli: 'zenity'`). macOS and Windows open their own dialogs by their full paths, so those tests run on Linux only.
- **The fake GitHub CLI** ([`e2e/fake-cli/fake-gh.cjs`](../e2e/fake-cli/fake-gh.cjs)) is installed with them as `gh` on every system (on Windows, the fake CLIs' executable under that name: it runs the fake GitHub CLI when started as `gh`, so one build serves all twelve names). It answers what sharing and a problem report's screenshots ask of `gh` (`--version`, `gist create`, `gist delete`, `api gists/<id>`), and the share dialog's sign-in (`auth login --web --hostname github.com`: its code and GitHub's device page, then signed in once the test writes `fake-github/approve`, kept as `fake-github/signed-in`, or failing on `fake-github/deny`), keeps its gists in the test's folder (`fake-github/`), logs its arguments (`cli: 'gh'`) and refuses everything else, so nothing reaches GitHub. AgentsView needs no fake program: `e2e/specs/sessions.spec.ts` starts a small HTTP server on 127.0.0.1 for it.
- **Before the tests**, [`e2e/global-setup.ts`](../e2e/global-setup.ts) builds the UI when a file it is built from is newer than the build, both as `npm run build` makes it (`dist/web`) and as a development build (`dist/web-dev`, `NODE_ENV=development`: React's StrictMode runs each effect twice, as under `npm run dev`) for the specs that serve it (`RELAY_WEB_DIR` set to `DEV_WEB_DIR` from `e2e/support/paths.ts`). `E2E_BUILD=always` builds both every run, `E2E_BUILD=never` never does (CI builds them in steps of their own, the same way). Then it installs the fakes, and works out once whether Harness Relay's own sandbox can work on this computer (`E2E_RELAY_SANDBOX`, for the workers).
- **Writing a test**: in `e2e/specs/<area>.spec.ts`, import `test` and `expect` from `../fixtures`, and the steps from [`e2e/support/app.ts`](../e2e/support/app.ts) (`startChat`, `send`, `confirmTeam`, `finishedReply`, `openTeamChat` for a chat with a team of its own, made through the API, `countPages` for the pages a tab loads, reloads included, and so on). For a test of each CLI, use the helpers of [`e2e/support/clis.ts`](../e2e/support/clis.ts) (`eachCli(CLIS, …)`, `chatWith()`, `eachHalf()` with `teamOf()`, `needsBelowFull()`), and take what differs from one CLI to another from its facts (`e2e/support/clis/<provider>.ts`), not from its name. A test that uses a CLI besides Claude Code, Codex and Grok outside those helpers installs it with `withClis()` in its `test.describe` (`withEveryCli()` for a test of them all); each one more installed makes every page of the test slower to open. With `E2E_SYSTEM_CLIS=1` (CI sets it) a CLI's own tests run only on the systems its facts name (`testedOn`: Linux always, and macOS or Windows where Harness Relay runs it differently there in a way its fake can tell, its file saying why), and are skipped elsewhere, saying where they run; without it, they run everywhere. A signed-in page starts on the home page (`homePage`); `openNewChat` opens the New chat page from the sidebar. Find elements by their role and visible text where the UI gives them (English unless the test changes the language), CSS classes only otherwise. Something new an agent should do goes in the fakes as a new directive (`DIRECTIVES` in `e2e/fake-cli/lib/plan.cjs`), which each CLI's module then plays in its own protocol.

Current specs (each scenario in [`e2e/README.md`](../e2e/README.md)):

- `e2e/specs/home.spec.ts` — the home page the app opens on, the sidebar's logo and name leading back to it, and its logo's animation: once, `transform` and `opacity` alone, none with reduced motion.
